  - *Expiry:* Not stated.
  - *Gotchas:* Free quotas and rate limits were not read. The free-access statement came from a search excerpt of startup.google.com/cloud, not a full page read (https://startup.google.com/cloud/).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup
- **edX audit track: free enrollment in most courses** (high) — Most edX courses can be audited for free before committing to the paid verified certificate track. Audit gives temporary access to select course content for the audit period. Graded assignments, exams and the certificate are not part of the audit track. The verified track is paid.
  - *Eligibility:* Any learner with a free edX account. Audit availability and access length are set per course (check the course page). No region or .edu limit found in the pages read.
  - *How to claim:* On edx.org click 'Register for free' or 'Create your free account'. Open the course and choose 'Continue with audit track' or 'Select audit track'.
  - *Expiry:* Audit access is temporary. Length varies by course.
  - *Gotchas:* Audit is not a certificate, and graded work is locked. Financial assistance requires enrolling in the audit track first (see the next finding). Homepage: https://www.edx.org/.
  - *Source:* https://www.edx.org/resources/how-to-audit-an-edx-class
- **edX financial assistance: up to 80% off the verified-track fee** (high) — Eligible learners can get up to an 80% reduction on the verified-track fee for eligible courses. Maximum five approvals per 12-month period. Each course needs its own application. Assistance applies only to the course it was granted for and cannot be transferred. Decisions come by email, within up to four weeks. Apply before the course's published cutoff to upgrade to verified.
  - *Eligibility:* Financial need, judged on income, employment status and need. Course-by-course: not every course accepts assistance, and a list of non-qualifying courses is published. No .edu requirement and no region limit found in the page read. Degree programs can use federal aid (FAFSA) instead.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Check the course homepage for assistance eligibility. 2) Enroll in the audit track. 3) Sign in to edX and complete the financial assistance application for that course. 4) Wait for the email decision (up to four weeks). 5) Upgrade to verified before the course's cutoff.
  - *Expiry:* Approval is per course. Cutoff varies by course. Five approvals per 12 months.
  - *Gotchas:* Cutoff dates vary, and missing one loses the discount. A denied application can be re-applied only if the error was in the submitted information. The homepage promo (code FALL2026, up to 20% off select courses and programs, 'for a limited time') is a discount, not a free offer.
  - *Source:* https://www.edx.org/resources/how-to-get-financial-assistance-for-edx-courses

### Figma for Education

- **Figma Starter plan (free-for-all individual plan)** (high) — Free tier available to anyone (students, educators, startups, individuals). Limits read on figma.com/pricing today: unlimited draft files shareable with unlimited viewers; co-editing with unlimited users on up to 3 files (max 3 pages per file); 30-day version history; 500 AI credits/month with a 150 AI credits/day cap; 10 variable modes; Figma Slides limited to 5 templates; community plugins only; basic Dev Mode inspection for viewers. Unlimited files/projects, team component libraries, full Dev Mode, advanced prototyping (motion publishing, high-res video export) require a paid plan (Professional+).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a Figma account. No credit card required (Figma's payment-method docs cover only paid subscriptions; sign-up is self-serve). No region exclusions noted for the free plan. No CC, no age gate beyond standard terms.
  - *How to claim:* Self-serve: create a Figma account at figma.com (Sign up → 'Get started for free'). No verification, no CC, no card. Free forever.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; free tier is permanent. Code layers are free through Nov 4, 2026 on paid plans, then consume AI credits.
  - *Gotchas:* Downgrading a paid team to Starter caps it at 3 files + 1 project; teams with 4+ files become view-only until files are moved to drafts. AI features consume the 500/mo credit pool (no rolver). 'Unlimited drafts' does not include a team space: shared collaboration is limited to 3 files.
  - *Source:* https://www.figma.com/pricing/(alsohttps://www.figma.com/pricing-faq/)
- **Figma for Education: Professional plan free (higher ed, high school, bootcamp & online-course students/educators)** (high) — Verified education status gives a free Education team with all Professional-plan functionality (per help.figma.com article read today): unlimited files/projects, team libraries, Dev Mode, advanced prototyping. Higher-ed and bootcamp/online-course users also get the AI toolkit (Figma Make, Figma agent) with monthly AI credits, and Figma Sites (each eligible user may publish ONE Site file; no custom domains). K-12/high-school education users do NOT get the AI tools or Sites features. FAQ: Education accounts valid 2 years for K-12 and higher education, 6 months for bootcamps (one 6-month extension max for bootcamp users).
  - *Eligibility:* Current student or educator in a structured classroom (high schools, universities, post-grad, trade schools, polytechnics, colleges, design/coding academies, selected online courses/bootcamps with curriculum-based mentor-led instruction). Must apply with a school-issued email where the school issues one; higher-ed verification runs through SheerID and can use student ID/government ID if no school email exists — so a .edu/domain email is NOT strictly required, but education status is tied to the verified email (changing the account email revokes status). Non-teaching school staff do not qualify; self-directed learners, startups, edtech companies, and nonprofits do not qualify. Available only in select regions (forum reports confirm 'not supported' errors from China). Must be old enough to consent; under-13 use belongs to the school-signed-terms route.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Have an existing Figma account under your school email (sign up with it, or change it before applying). 2) Go to figma.com/education/apply: role Student/Educator, institution type Higher Education / High School / Bootcamp or online course, click 'Verify your status' under the Professional card. 3) Higher ed: complete SheerID verification (school email or ID upload). 4) Bootcamp/online course: the provider admin must verify you (you cannot self-verify since Sept 2025); no action needed if your bootcamp is an approved partner. 5) Create an Education team. No credit card required — it is a free plan, not a trial.
  - *Expiry:* Higher ed / K-12 / high school: 2 years from verification, reapply when still enrolled/employed. Bootcamp: 6 months, renewable once for 6 more months.
  - *Gotchas:* Sept 2025 re-verification wave: users who did not re-verify by Sept 29, 2025 had Education teams converted to Starter (3 files / 1 project; 4+ files → everyone view-only). Changing your account email revokes verified status. Bootcamp users: initial 6 months + one 6-month extension only; no second extension, and you cannot reapply under the same email/account — some users have reportedly created a second account to re-verify. SheerID has strict ID-clarity rules and attempt limits. Some AI features (editor visual search) unavailable even to eligible education users. Education status ≠ paid-plan billing: nothing to pay, but you cannot buy seats or AI-credit add-ons on it.
  - *Source:* https://help.figma.com/hc/en-us/articles/360041061214-Figma-for-Education(alsohttps://www.figma.com/education/andhttps://www.figma.com/pricing-faq/)
- **Figma for Education: Enterprise plan free (K-12 districts and schools)** (high) — K-12 districts/schools get Figma Enterprise at no cost (the top tier), plus the school-signed-terms route required for students under 13 (school must contact Figma and sign school terms; under-13 use is not available via self-serve). Includes Google Workspace for Education integration (admin-managed licensing via App Licensing System), Google Classroom FigJam add-on, and Microsoft Education SAML SSO auto-provisioning. Figma and Google bring Figma + FigJam to US Chromebooks free. Education account validity for K-12: 2 years, reapply to renew. Free bi-weekly live training and on-site PD sessions available on request.
  - *Eligibility:* K-12 schools/districts (US-focused integrations; program available in select regions). Teachers and students 13+ can self-serve via the high-school route at figma.com/education/apply; any class with students under 13 requires the school to contact Figma (form/form at figma.com/education/k-12, education@figma.com) and sign school terms. Non-teaching staff excluded.
  - *How to claim:* 13+ students/teachers: figma.com/education/apply, select High School as institution type (K-12 educators selecting themselves use High School; district-wide deployment goes through the K-12 form). Under-13 classes: school submits the K-12 application form / emails education@figma.com, signs school terms; admins then deploy via Google/Microsoft integration. No credit card required.
  - *Expiry:* K-12 Education accounts valid 2 years, reapply to renew.
  - *Gotchas:* K-12/high-school education teams do NOT get Figma Make, the AI agent, or Figma Sites (higher-ed/bootcamp only). Region-limited: Figma for Education 'is only available in select regions'. Under-13 route is manual and takes longer (school terms, COPPA/FERPA handling). Google/Microsoft SSO provisioning means the school domain controls who gets access — a domain swap or SSO change can lock users out.
  - *Source:* https://www.figma.com/education/k-12/(alsohttps://www.figma.com/education/)
- **Figma for Startups — no live free offer (program appears ended/paused)** (high) — As of today, Figma's own site has no startup program: figma.com/startups returns 404, the pricing page lists no startup offer, and the official pricing FAQ explicitly lists 'Early stage Startups' among who does NOT qualify for free Education teams, and states 'We do not have any discounts for non-profits. Anyone is welcome to use Figma's free plan.' Third-party trackers (checked Sep 13–Oct 14, 2026) mark the formerly partner-gated 'Figma for Startups' offer (free Organization plan for 24 months for VC-backed startups via accelerator-partner referral; other listings describe up to 1 year of Professional at 100% off, a 50% discount for 6 months, or up to ~$1,000 in credits/50% discounts) as 'paused' or 'No Longer Available', with the application form no longer found on any Figma-owned page. Figma is also no longer listed in the GitHub Student Developer Pack. Startups today can only use the free Starter plan (or buy Professional seats, full $16/mo / dev $12/mo / collab $3/mo).
  - *Eligibility:* No eligibility path exists on Figma's own pages as of 2026-10-22. The legacy program (now unavailable) required: VC/accelerator backing or approved-partner referral, new Figma Organization customer, application with funding caps (<$10M in older listings), no CC required. Figma's official FAQ explicitly excludes early-stage startups from the free education program, and Figma does not give startup age/funding caps because it does not run a startup program.
  - *How to claim:* Not claimable: no application form exists on any Figma-owned page (figma.com/startups = 404). What remains: free Starter plan for the whole team (each person signs up free, no CC); check your accelerator/VC perk book, since the old offer was distributed only through partner referrals — if a partner still lists it, apply via that partner, but treat the current status as unverified.
  - *Gotchas:* Aggregator sites (Drifting Desk, StartupIntros) still show it as 'Active' with outdated terms — those are stale third-party pages, not Figma; Figma's own pages govern. The org-tier free offer (~$45/editor/mo list price x 24 months) was historically the most generous tier; no Enterprise-tier coverage existed. If you build a product on a partner perk listing, verify the referral pipeline (accelerator/bank perk catalog) still works before relying on it.
  - *Source:* https://www.figma.com/pricing-faq/(exclusionsreadtoday)+404athttps://www.figma.com/startups;third-partystatus:https://perkbook.co/startup-programs/figma-for-startups/('NoLongerAvailable')andhttps://guptadeepak.com/startup-offers/programs/figma-for-startups('Paused',checkedSep2026)
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 material changes: (1) September 5, 2025 — Figma Education plan update: Figma Make (AI) rolled out to the higher-ed Education plan; all higher-ed and high-school users were required to re-verify by Sept 29, 2025 or lose Professional features (teams downgraded to Starter: 3 files/1 project, 4+ files → view-only); bootcamp verification moved from self-verify to trusted-partner provider verification; bootcamp validity is 6 months with one 6-month extension. (2) Pricing restructure to a seat model (Full/Dev/Collab seats): Professional $16 full / $12 dev / $3 collab per month + 3,000/500/500 AI credits; Organization $55/$25/$5 annual; Enterprise $90/$35/$5 annual; Starter free with 500 AI credits/mo (150/day). This replaced the older flat per-editor pricing. (3) Time-boxed freebies being sunset: code layers free through November 4, 2026 (paid plans only; afterwards they consume AI credits); Figma Sites custom domains were free through 2025 with bandwidth/storage add-ons arriving 'in early 2026' — so education users' one-Site-allowance has no custom domain and paid tiers now face paid egress/domain extras. (4) Figma for Startups: ended/paused — figma.com/startups is a 404, no application form on any Figma-owned page as of Sep–Oct 2026 checks; third-party trackers mark it 'No Longer Available'/'Paused'; Figma's FAQ now explicitly excludes early-stage startups from free education teams. Legacy terms (for reference only): 24 months of the Organization plan free via accelerator partner referral, or up to $1,000 / 1 year Professional free / 50% off 6 months with <$10M funding caps. (5) GitHub Student Developer Pack no longer lists Figma among perks, so there is no third-party free Figma-Professional student channel. Not relevant but noted: a Figma for Government plan also exists (separate from education; not a student/startup offer).

### Gemini API Free Tier

- **Gemini API Free tier (Google AI Studio): free-of-charge models** (high) — Free input and output tokens, Google AI Studio access, and 'limited access to certain models' (official pricing page, fetched 2026-10-22). Models marked 'Free of charge' on the Free Tier column include Gemini 3.8 Flash (confirmed in raw text), Gemini 2.5 Pro, 2.5 Flash, 2.5 Flash-Lite, 3 Flash Preview, 3.1 Flash-Lite and other Flash, Live, TTS, transcription, Embedding 2 and Gemma 4 entries (a third-party count says 23 of 35 pricing-page entries are free). Not free: Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview, Gemini 4 Argon, Omni Flash, image models including 2.5 Flash Image, Veo 3.1, Lyria, 2.5 Pro Preview TTS, and grounding on Gemini 3+ models. Free-tier data: 'Used to improve our products' = Yes (paid tier = No). Numeric free-tier limits are NOT published in Google's docs: the rate-limits page says limits apply per project (not per API key), reset at midnight Pacific, are 'not guaranteed', and are shown per project in AI Studio (sign-in required). Third-party figures conflict (2.5 Pro 5 RPM / 100 RPD in several 2026 guides vs 50 RPD in a May 2026 guide; Flash-Lite 15 RPM / 1,000 RPD vs 30 RPM / 1,500 RPD). Treat numeric limits as unverified.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Google account to sign in to AI Studio. Official rate-limits table lists Free qualification as 'Active project or free trial'. No billing account or credit card is listed as required for Free tier; Tier 1 is the first tier that requires linking an active billing account. No .edu requirement, no startup age or funding cap. No country restriction appears on the captured pages (an older cached variant of the rate-limits page said 'Users in eligible countries'; unverified).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Open aistudio.google.com and sign in with a Google account. 2) Create an API key (AI Studio creates or uses a Google Cloud project). 3) Call a free-tier model such as gemini-2.5-flash or gemini-3.8-flash without linking billing. 4) Read the project's live RPM/TPM/RPD limits in AI Studio.
  - *Expiry:* No end date published for the free tier. The model lineup changes with model lifecycle (see sunset_notes).
  - *Gotchas:* Free-tier prompts and responses may be used to improve Google products; do not send private or regulated data. Spend-based limit is N/A on Free, but RPM/TPM/RPD overages return 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED. The $300 Google Cloud trial credit cannot pay Gemini API in AI Studio costs (official trial doc). Official billing tiers: Tier 1 = link active billing, billing tier cap $250; Tier 2 = $100 paid + 3 days, cap $2,000; Tier 3 = $1,000 paid + 30 days, cap $20,000 to $100,000+. Spend-based limits per rolling 10 minutes: Tier 1 $10, Tier 2 $50, Tier 3 $200. Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview and Gemini 4 Argon are paid-only.
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing
- **Vertex AI express mode (now 'Agent Platform in express mode'): card-free 90-day free tier** (high) — Free tier for new Google Cloud users for up to 90 days, within specified quotas, with no billing information required. Rate limits in express free tier: gemini-2.5-pro, gemini-2.5-flash, gemini-2.5-flash-lite and gemini-2.5-flash-image each max 10 requests per minute; gemini-3-pro-image, gemini-3.1-flash-image, gemini-3.1-pro-preview and gemini-3-flash-preview are 'Dynamic' (no fixed number published). Only the listed models are supported; dedicated Live API models (for example gemini-3.8-live) are not. Free-tier data source: Google Drive only. Separate from, and not available through, the Google Cloud Free Program or the $300 trial.
  - *Eligibility:* Developers with a @gmail.com Google Account. The 90-day free tier is for NEW Google Cloud users; existing Google Cloud users can sign up for express mode but use their existing billing account on the paid tier. No .edu requirement and no startup cap. Card-free in the free tier.
  - *How to claim:* In the Google Cloud console, open Express Mode, sign in with a @gmail.com account and accept the sign-up (APIs are enabled automatically and an API key is issued), then follow the Vertex AI Studio or API express-mode tutorial.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from sign-up. The free tier is removed if you are an existing Google Cloud user or after you enable billing (then standard pay-as-you-go limits apply).
  - *Gotchas:* Quotas are small (10 RPM on the 2.5 models; 3.x models have no published number). Product renamed: Vertex AI is now part of Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform (official Vertex AI release notes); docs and console names changed. Enabling billing ends the 90-day free tier.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/start/express-mode/overview
- **Google Cloud Free Trial: $300 Welcome credit for 90 days** (high) — $300 Welcome credit valid for 90 days from sign-up, spendable on most Google Cloud products including AI APIs, with Free Tier monthly limits available during the trial. Not charged during the trial unless you upgrade. The credit cannot pay Gemini API in AI Studio costs (use the Gemini API free tier or prepay) or third-party partner (MaaS) generative AI models.
  - *Eligibility:* Only if you have never been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform or Firebase and have not previously signed up for the Free Trial. A credit card or other payment method valid for the trial period is REQUIRED (not card-free); some countries also require bank-account verification; Google places an authorization hold typically released within a few days to a month. Country availability follows Google's supported-countries list (not fetched this run). No .edu requirement and no startup cap.
  - *How to claim:* Go to cloud.google.com/free and click 'Get started for free' (or console.cloud.google.com/freetrial). Sign up, add a payment method and complete verification; a Free Trial billing account preloaded with $300 is created. Upgrade later from the console Welcome page via 'Activate'.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from sign-up or until the $300 is spent, whichever comes first. Then the trial billing account closes, with a 30-day grace period before unrecovered resources are permanently deleted. Upgrading ends the trial; leftover credit keeps its 90-day expiry.
  - *Gotchas:* Not a card-free offer. Free Tier products require a billing account. The $300 trial does not disqualify you from the Start startup tier (that tier excludes only credits received beyond the free trial, per official wording). Free Tier overage during a trial is paid from the $300 credit, capped at $300 total.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/gcp-free-tier
- **Google Cloud Free Tier (Always Free monthly limits, 20+ products)** (high) — Free usage of select Google Cloud products up to specified monthly limits. Official cloud.google.com/free page: 'free usage of 20+ popular products, including Compute Engine and AI APIs'. Per-product monthly quotas were not verified in this run; earlier third-party quota figures were not confirmed.
  - *Eligibility:* A Google Cloud billing account is required to access the Free Tier (official docs). Paid billing accounts pay for any overage; Free Trial accounts draw overage from the $300 credit. No .edu requirement, no startup cap, no region limit captured.
  - *How to claim:* Create a billing account (through the Free Trial or a paid signup) and use the products within their monthly limits; monitor usage in the Billing console.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing; no expiry stated for Free Tier monthly limits.
  - *Gotchas:* A billing account is needed even for free usage. Exceeding monthly limits on a paid account incurs charges. Exact limits differ by product, so read each product's free-tier page before relying on them.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program: Start tier (up to $2,000)** (high) — Up to $2,000 USD in Google Cloud credits, valid for one year. Google Workspace Business Plus free for 12 months for new signups (listed in the official comparison). Official Start-tier description: digital-native startups with a working MVP, a clear business model, and plans to seek venture funding soon.
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 24 months; not yet received Google Cloud credits beyond the free trial. The listed criteria do not require a funding round. No .edu requirement. A card requirement is not stated on the captured program page; region not captured. Applications are reviewed and approved against eligibility; approval is not guaranteed.
  - *How to claim:* Click 'Apply now' on cloud.google.com/startup/benefits (/startup/apply) and complete the application. The form itself was not captured; third-party guides say it asks for a company website, a company-domain email and billing account details.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid for one year.
  - *Gotchas:* Small pool. Credits received beyond the free trial make you ineligible. Scale later requires having received no more than $5,000 in Google Cloud credits.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program: Scale tier (up to $200,000, coverage-based)** (high) — Up to $200,000 USD in Google Cloud credits, coverage-based (credit applies only to eligible usage): Year 1 = 100% of eligible usage up to $100,000; Year 2 = 20% of eligible usage up to an additional $100,000. Program credits cover Google's models including Gemini and Gemma. Also Google Workspace Business Plus free for 12 months (new signups) and '$600 monthly credits in Google Maps Platform' (official list; duration text truncated in capture). Official description: VC-funded startups ready to grow and scale.
  - *Eligibility:* Funding: raised Pre-Seed or Seed within the last 5 years, or Series A within the last 12 months. Qualifying investment: equity from institutional investors and VCs; SAFEs are eligible; Web3 token raises and blockchain foundation grants are accepted. Private equity, government innovation grants, prize funding, crowdfunding, angel, and friends and family funding do NOT qualify for Scale (evaluated for other tiers). Company: founded within the last five years (official; several third-party sites say 10 years). Operating in stealth is supported upon verification of funding. Prior credits: not more than $5,000 in Google Cloud credits received. No .edu requirement; a card requirement is not stated on the captured page.
  - *How to claim:* Apply from the official benefits page (/startup/apply) with funding round details (investor, round, date). Google reviews eligibility and approves at its discretion. Partner and accelerator routes are third-party guidance and unverified.
  - *Expiry:* Two-year coverage structure (Year 1 and Year 2 as above); no renewal stated.
  - *Gotchas:* Coverage-based, not a lump sum: you are not paid out for unused coverage, and Year 2 drops to 20% coverage. Third-party sources (Rho, Questd, getcloudcredits) give founding-age and tier amounts that differ from the official page; the official page is used here. Angel and friends-and-family money does not count for Scale.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program: AI-first track (up to $350,000)** (high) — Up to $350,000 USD in Google Cloud credits for AI startups: Year 1 = 100% of eligible usage up to $250,000; Year 2 = 20% of eligible usage up to an additional $100,000. Also dedicated AI training, resources and experts, and AI webinars and content (official benefits page).
  - *Eligibility:* Same funding and company rules as Scale. The official captured text does not define 'AI startup'; third-party guides say the startup must use or plan to use Vertex AI or Gemini as a foundation of its product (unverified). Startup age and funding cap as for Scale.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through the same Google for Startups Cloud Program application (/startup/apply); the AI-first tier is a higher tier within the same program and Google assigns it on review.
  - *Expiry:* Two-year coverage structure as for Scale.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits apply only to eligible usage. Overclaiming AI-first may land you in a lower tier (third-party guidance, unverified).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google Cloud for Education (students): 200 free Google Skills credits** (high) — 200 free Google Skills credits for free online labs, skill badges and courses; credits expire one year after issue. Also free Google Skills Arcade labs, a no-cost preview of Google Cloud Certificates on YouTube, and Developer Student Club chapters. These are learning and lab credits, not Google Cloud billing credits.
  - *Eligibility:* Students ('students can request access'). The captured page does not state a .edu email requirement or a student-verification method, nor any startup cap or card requirement. Region not captured.
  - *How to claim:* Open cloud.google.com/edu/students, click 'Request credits' (Google Skills), and sign up for the learning-program emails if desired; Developer Student Club chapters are listed on the page.
  - *Expiry:* Each credit expires one year after it is issued.
  - *Gotchas:* These are training credits for labs and courses, not billing credits for production cloud spend. Do not confuse them with the $300 Free Trial.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Cloud Teaching credits: faculty-only course credits** (high) — Google Cloud credits for courses, redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after the course start date and usable for up to 1 year after course start. Up to 3 courses per application. The official form does not state a per-course amount on the captured page (an older Google support article uses a $50 worked example, which is not a grant amount). Credits are for student hands-on learning; not for commercial or personal use and not for course infrastructure; crypto mining prohibited (older support article).
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only: students cannot apply directly but can ask faculty to apply. Available only in the countries listed on the official form (includes the United States and the United Kingdom among others). Older support article: US regionally accredited institutions plus degree-awarding institutions in eligible countries; for-profit institutions not supported. Expected course start date must be within 1 year of the application date. No .edu email rule stated; faculty status at an institution is required.
  - *How to claim:* Faculty submit the application at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching: personal and institution details, course name, expected start date, course abstract, and how credits will be used. Up to 3 courses. Only the most recent submission is reviewed.
  - *Expiry:* Redeem within 16 weeks after course start; credits valid for up to 1 year after course start.
  - *Gotchas:* Students cannot apply directly. Approval is discretionary (older support article says Google may change criteria at any time). Credits may not be used for commercial or personal use.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching/
- **Google Cloud research credits (Academic Research Grants): up to $5,000** (high) — Up to $5,000 in Google Cloud credits for research (official Cloud for Education listing: 'Apply for up to $5,000 in Google Cloud credits'). Credits provide computing power for research; they have no commercial value and may not be used for commercial purposes.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty, PhD students and postdoctoral researchers at accredited higher-education institutions, and eligible nonprofit research institutions, in approved countries (list not captured). Application requires a link to your profile in the institution's faculty or researcher directory and a link showing candidacy status. No startup cap; no .edu email rule stated (an institutional directory link is used instead).
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research with personal information, department, the directory link and candidacy-status link. Only the most recent submission is reviewed.
  - *Expiry:* Term and expiry were not captured on the page.
  - *Gotchas:* Non-commercial use only. The approved-country list was not captured, so check it before applying.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/
- **Gemini Academic Program: Gemini API credits and higher rate limits for researchers** (medium) — Qualified academic researchers can apply to receive Gemini API credits and higher rate limits for research projects. Named priority areas include evaluations and benchmarks, scientific discovery (for example rare diseases, materials science, sustainability), and embodiment and interactions. The credit amount is not stated on the captured page.
  - *Eligibility:* Qualified academic researchers (faculty, staff and PhD students) in supported countries (list not captured). A research project is required. No startup cap and no .edu-only rule captured.
  - *How to claim:* Open the 'Gemini Academic Program' section at ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/gemini-for-research and follow its application link (the link itself was not captured in this run).
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the captured page.
  - *Gotchas:* Amount is unpublished, and approval is not guaranteed. Aimed at research projects, not general student or coursework use.
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/gemini-for-research#gemini-academic-program
- **Google AI Pro free for 12 months: eligible US college students (consumer plan, not API credit)** (high) — One year of Google AI Pro free (valued at $19.99/month): 4x higher usage limits in Gemini, Gemini Spark, Gemini in Gmail and Google Docs, 5 TB storage, Google Health Premium and more (official Google blog, Aug 19, 2026). Footnote: 'Eligible students in the U.S. only. Offer Terms apply. Redeem until December 31, 2026. Valid form of payment required at sign-up. Unless cancelled earlier, Google AI Pro will automatically charge $19.99/month after the trial ends. Cancel anytime.' This is a consumer subscription and adds no Gemini API quota.
  - *Eligibility:* Eligible college students in the United States. Official student-hub text (search snippet, not fully fetched): ages 18+; new users and students whose 2025 AI Pro trial has expired can qualify; eligibility must be verified (method not captured). A .edu address requirement is not stated in the 2026 captured text (the 2025 student offer required a .edu address per The Verge, April 2025). No startup cap.
  - *How to claim:* Open gemini.google/students (student hub) and claim 'one year of Google AI Pro'; enter a valid payment method at sign-up and complete student verification when prompted.
  - *Expiry:* Free for 12 months; redeem by December 31, 2026; then auto-charged $19.99/month unless cancelled. Cancel anytime.
  - *Gotchas:* A payment method IS required even though the year is free, and it auto-renews at $19.99/month after the year. A Romania-locale capture of gemini.google/students showed a different offer (Google AI Plus, 26.99 RON/month after trial), so offers vary by market.
  - *Source:* https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/products/gemini-app/student-offer-google-ai/
- **Google AI Plus free for 12 months: eligible students outside the US (consumer plan)** (high) — One year of Google AI Plus free: 2x higher usage limits in Gemini, 400 GB storage, Gemini Omni access (official blog, Aug 19, 2026). Footnote: offered to 'eligible students in over 140 markets where Google AI Plus is available, except for in the U.S., Bolivia, Albania, Canada, Macau, Hong Kong, and Tunisia'. Consumer subscription; not API credits.
  - *Eligibility:* Eligible college students outside the U.S. in the listed markets (age and .edu requirement not captured). No startup cap.
  - *How to claim:* Claim via gemini.google/students; a valid payment method is required at sign-up.
  - *Expiry:* Free for 12 months; redeem-by date is in December 2026 (exact day cut off in capture; the Romania-locale page says 31 Dec 2026). After the trial, $4.99/month or the local-currency equivalent is charged automatically unless cancelled.
  - *Gotchas:* Market exclusions as listed. Local-currency pricing varies (Romania locale capture: 26.99 RON/month). Payment method required.
  - *Source:* https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/products/gemini-app/student-offer-google-ai/
- **Gemini for Education: free for accredited institutions (institution-level)** (medium) — Institutions can get Gemini for Education and NotebookLM free of charge by signing up for Google for Education, even if they use another productivity suite; gives default access to premium models, enterprise data protection and admin-managed access, included in Workspace for Education editions at no charge (official page). Google blog (Sep 8, 2025, search snippet): all accredited higher-education institutions can access Gemini for Education with data protections free of charge; more than 1,000 US institutions had integrated it, reaching over 10 million students.
  - *Eligibility:* Accredited higher-education institutions (institution-level, admin-managed). Individual students do not sign up on their own through this route, per the captured text. No .edu email rule stated; an institutional account is required.
  - *How to claim:* An institution admin requests an advisory session or signs up for Google for Education (edu.google.com), then turns on Gemini for Education in the Admin console.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated. The official page says higher limits than consumer tiers are coming ('soon with significantly higher limits'), which implies paid tiers for heavier use.
  - *Gotchas:* Institution-managed rather than individual. Higher limits will be a paid option. Official page says data is not used to train AI models.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/intl/ALL_us/gemini-built-for-education-higher-ed/
- **Google AI for Education Accelerator: free AI training and Career Certificates (US college students)** (medium) — Free AI training and Google Career Certificates for every college student in America, part of Google's AI education commitment; over 100 colleges and universities had signed up at announcement (Google blog, Aug 6, 2025, search snippet and partial fetch).
  - *Eligibility:* US college students, through participating institutions.
  - *How to claim:* Access through a participating institution or through Google Career Certificates; details at the blog link (full page not fetched in this run).
  - *Expiry:* Not stated.
  - *Gotchas:* Training and certificates, not cloud or API credits. Availability depends on institution participation.
  - *Source:* https://blog.google/products-and-platforms/products/gemini/google-ai-pro-students-learning/
- **2025–26 change:** (1) Gemini 2.5 Flash Image (gemini-2.5-flash-image) is deprecated and shuts down October 2, 2026 per the official pricing page; migrate to Gemini 3.1 Flash Image or 3.1 Flash Lite Image. It was not on the free tier. (2) Third-party guides (apiyi, findskill, April and May 2026) say that from April 1, 2026 Pro models were removed from the free tier, mandatory spending caps applied, and prepaid billing applied to new accounts; and that free-tier quotas were cut 50 to 80 percent in December 2025. The official pricing page captured 2026-10-22 still lists Gemini 2.5 Pro as Free of charge and Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview as not available on free, so the Pro-removal claim is only partly consistent with official data. The spend-cap idea matches the official rate-limit table's billing tier caps, but those caps are for paid tiers. (3) Vertex AI was folded into Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform (official Vertex AI release notes); express-mode docs now use 'Agent Platform' naming. (4) The 2025 US student offer (Google AI Premium / AI Pro, .edu address required per The Verge, April 2025; sign-up deadline extended to October 6, 2025 per the August 6, 2025 Google blog) has expired. The 2026 US offer redeems until December 31, 2026. (5) Startup tier terms differ across third-party sites (Scale founding age 10 years vs official five years; AI-first Year 1 amounts). The official cloud.google.com/startup/benefits page was used throughout. (6) Unverified: a search snippet attributed to a Google AI Developers Forum thread said the Gemini API would stop accepting unrestricted API keys from June 19, 2026. The fetched copy of that thread did not contain this text, so it is unconfirmed; restricting keys to the Gemini API is still advisable. (7) The $300 / 90-day Google Cloud trial and the Start tier $2,000 / 1-year credits match the official pages captured today; no 2025 or 2026 change to them was found. (8) No country list was captured for the Gemini API free tier; the teaching credits, research grants, Gemini Academic Program and Google AI Plus offers each carry their own country lists. (9) No free offer was found that is specifically labeled as a Gemini API startup credit; startup credits can be spent on Gemini through the Cloud program.

### GitHub

- **GitHub Copilot Free (perpetual free tier, no credit card)** (high) — $0/month, no subscription or payment/credit card required. Includes up to 2,000 code completions/month, a (not numerically disclosed) allowance of GitHub AI Credits (1 AI credit = $0.01 USD), limited chat and agent usage via auto model selection only, Copilot CLI and the Copilot app. Under the legacy request-based system this was 50 premium requests/month; after the 2026-06-01 migration to AI-credit usage billing GitHub does not publish a fixed credit figure for the Free plan. Not available to users whose account is managed by an org/enterprise; personal use only. Never expires; limits reset monthly.
  - *Eligibility:* Any individual with a GitHub account not managed by an organization/enterprise and without an existing Copilot paid/Student/teacher/maintainer plan. No email domain, age, or region criteria stated. No credit card required. Region-limited only where Copilot is unavailable (standard Copilot availability restrictions apply).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up directly in VS Code/Visual Studio ('Sign up for Copilot Free'), via github.com/settings/copilot ('Start using Copilot Free'), or chat at github.com/copilot. No verification steps.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual; monthly caps reset. Free/Student/teacher plans are reevaluated only where verification applies (Free has no verification, so no expiry).
  - *Gotchas:* Auto model selection only — no manual model picker, entry-level models only. Hitting the cap prompts an upgrade to Pro ($10). If you later join an org that assigns Copilot, Free access is lost. No code review, cloud agent, app modernization, or third-party agent delegation included. 'Unlimited completions' applies only to paid plans; Free caps at 2,000.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/features/copilot/plans
- **GitHub Copilot Student (free education license for verified students)** (high) — Free for students verified through GitHub Education: unlimited code completions and next-edit suggestions (completions never consume credits), plus a monthly GitHub AI Credits allowance — community-reported at 200 credits/month (~$2.00 of agent/chat usage) since the 2026-06-01 credit-based billing; GitHub does not publish a single official dollar figure for students, so verify in your plan's usage display. Limited chat/agent usage, auto model selection only. Since 2026-03-12 students are on a dedicated 'Copilot Student plan' (UI shows it alongside GitHub Education benefits); premium models incl. GPT-5.4, Claude Opus and Sonnet are no longer self-selectable (GPT-5.3-Codex removed from the picker 2026-04-27); access to OpenAI/Anthropic/Google models continues via Auto routing. Eligibility is reevaluated monthly by GitHub.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13+ enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting course at an accredited institution. Verification is via school-issued email and/or official, dated proof of enrollment (no strict .edu requirement, but .edu helps). No credit card required. Global program (thousands of universities). Not available to managed org accounts.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at github.com/settings/education/benefits ('Start an application', complete verification — school email/proof). Once verified, under 'Free GitHub developer resources for students and teachers' click Learn more and activate Copilot Student (or github.com/github-copilot/free_signup). Activation can lag verification by a few days; do not complete a paid checkout while waiting.
  - *Expiry:* Persists while academic verification remains valid; GitHub reevaluates eligibility every month — access is withdrawn when no longer enrolled/verified. Model/limit adjustments are being rolled out in stages (GitHub said more model/limit changes would follow the 2026-03 transition).
  - *Gotchas:* Credit allowance (~200 credits / ~$2) is small: agent-mode sessions with newer models can exhaust it in one day (community reports of 4-20 agent requests using the full allocation post-June 2026). Auto model selection only, no manual picker. No third-party agents, code review, or cloud agent included. ~2M students use the program; GitHub is actively trimming it for sustainability.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/copilot/how-tos/manage-your-account/free-access-with-copilot-student
- **GitHub Copilot Pro free for verified teachers and open-source maintainers (education/OSS license)** (high) — Full Copilot Pro at $0: unlimited completions, model selection, chat/agent/CLI, code review and cloud agent, and a $15/month AI-credit allowance (Base 1,000 + Flex 500 = 1,500 AI credits, 1 credit = $0.01). Two qualifying paths: (1) verified teacher on GitHub Education, (2) maintainer of a popular open-source repository (eligibility is determined by GitHub; objective repo thresholds are not publicly published — GitHub directs you to Copilot settings to see if you qualify).
  - *Eligibility:* Teachers: faculty at an accredited educational institution, verified via GitHub Education. OSS maintainers: maintainers of 'popular' open-source repos as judged by GitHub's criteria. Eligibility reevaluated monthly by GitHub.
  - *How to claim:* Teachers: apply/verify at github.com/settings/education/benefits, then activate in Copilot settings ('Get access to GitHub Copilot'). Maintainers: open Copilot settings; if eligible, a 'GitHub Copilot Pro' eligibility page appears — click Get access and save use policies. No payment info required for the free grant.
  - *Expiry:* While eligibility holds (monthly reevaluation). Note: if you are granted this free access you cannot cancel the plan — only losing eligibility ends it.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Popular' OSS-maintainer criteria are opaque; if you don't see the offer you likely don't qualify. Free access is personal, not shareable to the team. If you later join an organization that assigns Copilot seats, the free grant may be displaced.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-on-github/set-up-copilot/enable-copilot/set-up-for-teachers-and-os-maintainers
- **GitHub Education / Student Developer Pack benefits bundled with Copilot Student** (high) — Verified students also get a free GitHub Pro account (unlimited private repos with advanced collaboration), 3,000 GitHub Actions minutes/month, up to 180 GitHub Codespaces core hours/month (plus ~20 GB Codespaces storage), 2 GB GitHub Packages storage, and the Student Developer Pack (dozens of third-party partner offers for professional dev tools). These are GitHub's education-license companions to Copilot Student; the Copilot benefit is claimed from the same GitHub Education portal.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13+ enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting program at an accredited institution; apply with school-issued email and/or official dated proof of enrollment. Check first whether your school is a GitHub Campus Program partner (in that case benefits may be pre-arranged). No credit card required.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via education.github.com / github.com/settings/education/benefits, complete verification, then claim offers under 'Free GitHub developer resources for students and teachers' in that portal.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to active student verification (reevaluated monthly). Codespaces hours/Actions minutes reset monthly; unclaimed Codespaces hours are lost, not banked.
  - *Gotchas:* Actions minutes and Codespaces hours are per-month allowances that reset — no rollover. Usage in class/teacher-owned repos bills the owning org instead. 180 core-hours figure is what the docs state ('up to 180 core hours'); exact storage figures come from the marketing page.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/education/students
- **Trial credits / startup program: none GitHub-side for Copilot** (medium) — No time-limited free trial of paid Copilot plans is offered on the current plans page (the $0 Free plan is the entry point, not a trial), and GitHub does not run a GitHub Copilot startup program, startup credit pool, or funded 'Copilot for startups' offer. The nearest adjacent program is Microsoft's Cloud Startups initiative (Azure credits for eligible startups; GitHub is a Microsoft company, but Copilot Free/Student/Pro are not part of it and no Copilot-specific startup benefit was found). Confirmed by absence across the official plans, docs, and education pages reviewed today; web-search budget was exhausted before independently verifying Microsoft Cloud Startups terms.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — no GitHub Copilot startup offer exists to claim.
  - *How to claim:* n/a.
  - *Expiry:* n/a.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not expect 'startup credit' or 'Copilot Pro trial' offers that third-party activation/activation-marketplace sites advertise (e.g., shared-account 'Copilot activation' services) — those are unofficial and violate ToS. The free path is strictly Free plan, verified-student plan, or teacher/OSS-maintainer Pro.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/features/copilot/plans
- **GitHub Pro (free for students)** (high) — Free GitHub Pro while verified as a student: 3,000 GitHub Actions min/month, 180 Codespaces core-hrs/month, 2GB GitHub Packages, 20GB Codespaces storage. Core of the pack; most-featured benefit.
  - *Eligibility:* GitHub account owned by a student verified through GitHub Education (school/.edu email or enrollment proof). Must be enrolled; benefits renew while student status is maintained.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a GitHub account owned by the student. 2) Verify as a student at education.github.com (school email or enrollment docs). 3) Pro benefits are applied automatically to the account once verified; manage via the pack page.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while verified; re-verify as enrollment changes. Not a fixed trial.
  - *Gotchas:* Actions minutes beyond 3,000/mo are billable; Codespaces overage beyond 180 core-hrs incurs charges. Benefits lapse if student verification lapses.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Copilot (Student plan)** (high) — Copilot Student plan included free: unlimited code completions, an allowance of GitHub AI Credits for chat/agent/CLI/Spaces, and limited chat/agent usage restricted to auto model selection. More generous than the base Copilot Free tier (which is 2,000 completions/mo + a limited AI-credit allowance, 1 credit = $0.01, models incl. Haiku 4.5 / GPT-5 mini, no CC required).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via GitHub Education (the pack). Student plan is a dedicated tier; verified students link to the student offer from the copilot features page.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Verify student status in GitHub Education. 2) Activate the Copilot Student plan from the pack / Copilot page. 3) Use in editor, CLI, web, or Claude Code/agents.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to student/enrollment status; renews while verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Student plan chat/agent is capped and limited to auto model selection (vs. Pro/Pro+ choice). 2026: from ~Apr 24 GitHub may use Free/Pro/Pro+ interaction data for model training unless you opt out; from Jun 1, 2026 code-review workflows start consuming Actions minutes.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Codespaces (free Pro-level)** (high) — Free Pro-level access to Codespaces anywhere in the account, as part of GitHub Pro for students (supports the 180 core-hrs/mo + 20GB storage bundle).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Included automatically with GitHub Pro once student-verified; create and run Codespaces directly.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Compute hours beyond the 180 core-hrs/mo are billed; large machine sizes burn hours faster. Idle machines keep accruing hours.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Pages** (high) — One site per account and per organization, plus unlimited project sites, free.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GitHub user; in the pack context available to verified students.
  - *How to claim:* Available by default; deploy a site from a repo (gh-pages) or project site from a repo settings tab.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing; free tier with standard bandwidth limits.
  - *Gotchas:* Standard free-tier bandwidth limits apply; no AI/LLM features here.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Desktop** (high) — Open-source desktop Git client, free for everyone (not student-specific).
  - *Eligibility:* Any user; listed in the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Download and install from github.com/desktop; sign in with a GitHub account.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing, free open source.
  - *Gotchas:* Requires a working GitHub account; no trial/CC involved.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Microsoft Azure for Students (18+)** (high) — $100 Azure credit usable within 12 months, plus free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (new customers), plus 65+ always-free services. (Live page today shows a single flat student tier, not the older 'monthly $-credit' structure some sources still cite.)
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time university students 18+; sign up with a school email; renew yearly while enrolled; no credit card required; one account per person.
  - *How to claim:* 1) From the pack, open the Azure for Students claim link. 2) Sign up with school email. 3) Receive $100 credit + free monthly service amounts; renew yearly.
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit valid 12 months; free monthly amounts for 12 months; renew while a student.
  - *Gotchas:* Beyond free amounts and the $100 credit, metered/egress charges apply. Region availability varies. Always-free tiers have their own caps (e.g. 750 B1 hrs, 32GB storage).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students/
- **Microsoft Azure for Students (ages 13-17)** (medium) — For students aged 13-17: free access to a subset of Azure services (App Services, Functions, Notification Hubs, MySQL in-app, Application Insights, Azure DevOps). No credit card required.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13-17 via GitHub Education; separate tier from the 18+ offer.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's 13-17 Azure link; no CC.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while enrolled in the 13-17 tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Service subset only; not the full 18+ catalog; no $ credit tier for this age band.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Visual Studio Dev Essentials (incl. $200 Azure credit)** (medium) — Visual Studio Community + Pluralsight training, and '1 free year of Azure services with $200 Azure credit for the 1st month' as listed in the pack.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Dev Essentials link; sign in with the student/educational account.
  - *Expiry:* ~1 year of Azure services; credit for the first month.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit and free-service terms are set by Microsoft/VS and can change; metered overage beyond the free year is billable.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Azure for Startups (startup offer, not in SDC pack)** (high) — For the 'Startup' front: $1,000 Azure credits instantly with no application, and up to $5,000 total credits after business verification. Initial credits valid 90 days; post-verification credits valid 180 days.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startup (business), NOT the student program; separate from the GitHub Student Developer Pack. Business verification required for the larger tier.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at Azure for Startups; instant $1,000 tier, then complete business verification for up to $5,000.
  - *Expiry:* $1,000 credits 90 days; up-to-$5,000 credits 180 days post-verification.
  - *Gotchas:* Not available to the SDC student pack; it is a distinct startup program with its own criteria and verification.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students/
- **Heroku** (medium) — $13 USD/month for 24 months (Heroku platform subscription discount).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack; valid payment/claim method required by Heroku.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Heroku link; create Heroku account and apply the 24-month offer.
  - *Expiry:* 24 months. A 24-month committed/discounted subscription, not free-forever.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a 24-month discounted subscription that can auto-continue past 24 months unless cancelled - read renewal terms. Add-ons and extra resources are billed separately.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Appwrite (Education plan)** (medium) — Education plan: 2 projects at Pro-equivalent limits ($40/month value) for 6 months.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the 6-month education plan through the pack's Appwrite link.
  - *Expiry:* 6 months.
  - *Gotchas:* After 6 months, the plan reverts to free-tier limits or paid; egress/overage beyond included limits can cost.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **LocalStack** (medium) — Free license to LocalStack's AWS emulator in a ready-to-use cloud environment.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the free LocalStack license through the pack link.
  - *Expiry:* Per LocalStack's student terms (verify).
  - *Gotchas:* Check current license/region availability; cloud instance may have free-tier resource caps.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Stripe** (medium) — $25 in Stripe credits to offset fees on Stripe Payments, Billing and other products.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the $25 credit through the pack's Stripe link.
  - *Expiry:* Credit validity per Stripe terms (verify).
  - *Gotchas:* Credits offset fees only; Stripe also charges per-transaction fees on top of covered usage; verify credit expiry.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Clerk** (medium) — Free Clerk Pro plan while a student (auth/UI, more limits than free tier).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the free Pro plan through the pack's Clerk link.
  - *Expiry:* While student status is maintained.
  - *Gotchas:* Reverts to free/paid tier after student status ends; confirm no CC is required at signup.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Sentry** (medium) — Free tier: 50K errors, 100K transactions, 1GB attachments, 500 replays, Team features, 1-year limit (renewable).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Sentry link; renew annually while a student.
  - *Expiry:* 1 year, renewable while student.
  - *Gotchas:* Beyond the error/transaction/replay caps, ingestion is blocked or downgraded; renew or lose access.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **MongoDB Atlas** (medium) — $50 MongoDB Atlas credits, plus access to MongoDB Compass and MongoDB University incl. a free certification valued at $150.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the $50 credit + tools/cert through the pack's MongoDB link.
  - *Expiry:* $50 credit window per MongoDB terms (verify).
  - *Gotchas:* Atlas cluster costs beyond the credit/MB free tier and egress are billable; free shared clusters have storage/network caps.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Datadog** (medium) — Pro account including 10 hosts/servers, free for 2 years.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the 2-year Datadog Pro through the pack's Datadog link.
  - *Expiry:* 2 years; renew per Datadog terms.
  - *Gotchas:* Beyond 10 hosts or included log/metric volume, charges apply; 2-year term then re-evaluates.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Camber** (medium) — Free Student plan while enrolled: 40 CPU hours, 5 GPU hours, 50GB storage, 50 agent messages/month.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the Camber student plan through the pack's link.
  - *Expiry:* While enrolled as a student.
  - *Gotchas:* Hour/message caps; GPU hours are scarce - large jobs consume them quickly; overage billed after caps.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **CARTO** (medium) — Free upgraded account for 2 years: increased storage, real-time data, Location Data Services credits, premium features.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the 2-year CARTO account through the pack's link.
  - *Expiry:* 2 years.
