- **50% student discount on Microsoft 365 Personal with Copilot (Word/Excel/PPT/OneNote/Outlook + Copilot, 1 TB OneDrive)** (medium) — As read 2026-10-22 (offer cards on MS education hub and store/education page): 'Use your college email to save 50% on Microsoft 365 with Copilot included' — Microsoft 365 Personal with Copilot at 50% off the list price, includes desktop apps with Copilot, 1 TB OneDrive, Defender security. Offer card title on store page: 'Students save 50% on Microsoft 365 Personal'. This replaced/superseded the old '50% off' Office 365 University offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time and part-time enrolled university/college students at accredited institutions; verification of student status + a valid Microsoft account (personal MSA, NOT the .edu address, for the subscription itself). .edu/college email used to verify. Available in the markets where the student deal is offered (US-first, expanded in Oct 2025 'to more markets around the world').
  - *How to claim:* 1) microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365/college-student-pricing. 2) Create/sign in with personal Microsoft account. 3) Add payment info (a payment method IS required to subscribe). 4) Provide EDU email for verification. 5) Email with completion link arrives at the student mailbox; subscribe at 50% off.
  - *Expiry:* Recurring subscription: auto-renews at the discounted monthly/annual price while verification holds; eligibility can be re-checked (Microsoft periodically re-verifies student status; account downgrades when no longer eligible).
  - *Gotchas:* Payment method required (unlike Azure for Students). It's a personal MSA subscription, not a school license. Trial vs free: the discounted plan is a paid subscription from day one — the free 12-month variant (below) is the no-cost path where available.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365/college-student-pricing
- **Free 12 months of Microsoft 365 Personal (with Copilot) for US college students — announced Sep 4, 2025; 50% off afterward** (medium) — Per Microsoft's White House AI Education commitments announcement (2025-09-04): 'Microsoft 365 Personal free for 12 months to every college student in the United States', incl. community college students; Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OneNote, Outlook with Copilot built in; sign-up opened immediately 'through October 31, 2025 (valid university email address required)'. After the free year: eligible for 50% discount to continue (list ~$99.99/yr before discount). Microsoft's Oct 1, 2025 announcement 'expanding the limited-time student offer to more markets around the world'. The education Office page (2026-10-22) still shows a 'Free for students: Our ultimate AI and productivity subscription' section pointing to college-student-pricing, but the live page could not be read today (JS/timeout), so current (post-Oct-2025-window) availability is unconfirmed.
  - *Eligibility:* US college students (incl. community colleges) at launch; later expanded to more markets. Valid university email required; signup on a personal Microsoft account. No credit card for the free year is advertised; a payment method is required for post-expiry continuation at the discounted price.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up via the M365 student offer (college-student-pricing / 'Free for students' card on education pages). 2) Verify with valid university email. 3) Use M365 Personal free for 12 months; set a reminder before the free year ends to switch to 50% discount or cancel.
  - *Expiry:* Free period = 12 months per claim; original US signup window closed Oct 31, 2025 (international expansion Oct 2025). Status of the offer in Oct 2026 could not be confirmed from the live page today.
  - *Gotchas:* Limited-time offer: likely lapses or narrows between promo windows; check the page before relying on it. Auto-billing kicks in after the free year unless cancelled. 1 TB OneDrive included; Copilot usage limits apply.
  - *Source:* https://blogs.microsoft.com/on-the-issues/2025/09/04/new-white-house-commitments/
- **Microsoft Store 10% student/parent discount on select devices** (high) — As read 2026-10-22: 'Students and parents can get 10% off on select devices, including Microsoft Surface' (education hub) — store FAQ wording: 'Use the Microsoft student discount to get 10% off select physical products, including select Surface devices, as well as select student laptops, and PC accessories'. Ongoing, not time-boxed.
  - *Eligibility:* Students: school-issued email 'provided by the school (for example, contoso.edu) that can receive external email, be of legal age to sign up for an online offer'. Parents discount is the separate parent-verification variant of the same program. US storefront.
  - *How to claim:* 1) microsoft.com/en-us/store/b/education. 2) Sign in, verify student (school email) or parent status. 3) 10% applied to eligible SKUs at purchase.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing while eligible; no stated end date.
  - *Gotchas:* Only 'select' SKUs; Microsoft 'reserves the right to verify an eligible retailer's advertised price and availability'. Physical products only (no M365 subscription discounts here).
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/store/b/education
- **Azure Dev Tools for Teaching (Azure Education Hub) — free professional dev tools, IDEs & Git/CI tooling for students and faculty** (medium) — Free, non-credit offering: Windows 11/10 Pro, Visual Studio, SQL Server, Windows Server, Project Professional, Visio Professional (title list confirmed by UTEP's Feb 2026 catalog and U of I WebStore listing; full list in the Microsoft FAQ) plus GitHub Student Developer Pack integration on campus. 'Software is acquired ... only for instructional and research purposes'; commercial use prohibited (except free Windows-store apps). Office is NOT included. No expiry while enrolled — re-verifying student/faculty status keeps access; the 2016 'no expiry date' model continues.
  - *Eligibility:* Students and faculty/staff of accredited institutions; student status verified with the university email via the Azure Education Hub (institutional SSO). Region: worldwide, campus-by-campus agreements.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Open the Azure Education Hub student-verification portal (linked from your campus IT page). 2) Sign in with your university email (SSO where available). 3) After verification, browse 'Software' in the hub and download titles (license terms per title).
  - *Expiry:* Licensed for as long as enrolled/employed at the qualifying institution; no personal expiry date; access lapses on verification failure/graduation. Titles change with Microsoft's licensing agreements.
  - *Gotchas:* Non-commercial, instructional/research use only — cannot run departmental infrastructure or commercial products. Microsoft's own page (azure.microsoft.com/en-us/education) now funnels to Azure-for-Students content; program details live in the hub FAQ. Credit: no Azure credit in the hub — the $100 student credit is separate (Azure for Students).
  - *Source:* https://www.utep.edu/technologysupport/servicecatalog/onl_azureducation.html
- **Azure for Startups (new 2025/2026 credit program) — $1,000 instant credits, up to $5,000 total; card required for identity** (high) — As read 2026-10-22 on the Azure-for-Students page (a new tier now advertised alongside the student offer): 'Azure for Startups: $1,000 in Azure credits instantly — no application required; unlock up to $5,000 in total credits with business verification; credits valid for 90 days (initial) and 180 days (post-verification); access to full Azure catalog up to credit limits; spending protection — credit card required only for identity verification and services beyond credit; no upfront commitment — cancel anytime; move to pay-as-you-go after credits expire or are used.'
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers starting a startup (any new business customer can get the $1,000 instant tier; the extended tier requires business verification). Not age/funding-capped like the legacy Founders Program. Worldwide where Azure is available.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a new Azure account; select the Azure for Startups plan (ad on the Azure free/students pages). 2) No application for the instant $1,000 — card added for identity verification. 3) Complete business verification to unlock up to $4,000 more (total $5,000). 4) Set spending limits; credits burn down 90 days (initial) / 180 days (post-verification).
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire on fixed clocks: 90 days (initial $1,000) and 180 days (post-verification pool). After expiry: pay-as-you-go unless cancelled.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required (identity + beyond-credit charges) — unlike Azure for Students. 90/180-day validity is far shorter than legacy Founders Program credits. Not a 'trial': unused credits are forfeit, not refunded.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Microsoft for Startups Founders Program — 'up to $150,000' Azure credits marketing (legacy big program)** (medium) — As read 2026-10-22: the Microsoft for Startups landing page still advertises 'up to $150,000 in credits' — 'Credits can be applied across eligible Azure services', with AI tools ('scalable AI tools that help cut costs'), technical resources, and the startup network. The 'Get started now' CTA now routes to the Azure Portal startup home view (portal.azure.com/?homeview=startup) rather than a standalone Founders Program application page; /startups/founders 404s. No numeric eligibility thresholds (funding cap, startup age, region) are published on the current page — detailed terms now live behind the portal flow. Legacy Founders Program historically: early-stage startups, < $30M raised, multi-month credit validity, plus training/mentorship/benefits; those specifics are not confirmable on the live pages today.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startups building on Microsoft tech (funding cap and age limits historically: < $30M raised; regions with Azure availability). No current live-page confirmation of caps today.
  - *How to claim:* 1) microsoft.com/startups → 'Get started now' (fwlink resolves to portal.azure.com/?homeview=startup). 2) Start in the Azure Portal startup flow / register via the startup program portal; larger credit pools historically required application + investor/partner referral; the new instant tier is the Azure for Startups $1k/$5k above.
  - *Expiry:* Credit validity depends on the specific granted pool (legacy grants were ~6–12 month usability); not published on the landing page today.
  - *Gotchas:* The $150K figure is a marketing maximum (historically: up to $150K Azure credits including base grants); most startups receive far less, tiered by review. The classic application-based Founders flow appears to have been consolidated into the portal 'Azure for Startups' tiers; treat the $150K as aspirational ceiling, not entitlement.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/startups
- **College PC bundle promo (2026) — 1 yr Microsoft 365 Premium + 1 yr Xbox Game Pass Ultimate + controller; promotion ENDED** (high) — Offer valid 4/15/2026–8/31/2026, redemption deadline 9/30/2026 (per official FAQ read 2026-10-22: 'This promotion has ended'): eligible US college students buying a new eligible Windows 11 PC got 1 year of Microsoft 365 Premium (ARV $199.99), 1 year Xbox Game Pass Ultimate (ARV $275.88), and an Xbox Design Lab controller (ARV $79.99) — '$500+ in added value'. One PC per student/account; refurbished PCs excluded; controller US shipping only.
  - *Eligibility:* US college/university students at accredited US institutions with a valid .edu email; eligible new Windows 11 PC purchase; M365 Premium: new subscribers or Personal/Family upgraders; Game Pass: new subscribers only.
  - *How to claim:* Ended: (1) buy eligible PC by 8/31/26; (2) verify .edu status via on-PC prompt or email at the Microsoft Academic Verification page; (3) claim subs + controller promo code by 9/30/26 11:59pm PT. A valid payment method was required; after 1 year both subs auto-renew at monthly fees unless cancelled at account.microsoft.com. Game Pass had to be verified within 5 days of activation.
  - *Expiry:* Ended: offer window closed 8/31/2026; redemption closed 9/30/2026. Page remains live for redemptions of in-window purchases.
  - *Gotchas:* Payment method required; auto-renewal charges after year 1; new-subscriber-only rules for the two subs; this is a hardware-tied promo, not a standalone free offer.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/back-to-school-offer
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit + free monthly service allowances** (high) — Verified today on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students: $100 Azure credit usable on most Azure products within 12 months; free monthly amounts on 20+ popular services for 12 months (new customers only), e.g. 750 hrs B1s/burstable VMs (Linux & Windows), 750 hrs PostgreSQL/MySQL Flexible Server B1MS + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup, 720 vCore-hrs Azure SQL Managed Instance, 1 Azure Container Registry (Standard, 100 GB), 5,000 transactions of Azure Vision in Foundry Tools; free monthly amounts on 65+ always-free services with no time limit, e.g. Azure Functions 1M requests/mo, App Service 10 web/API apps + 1 GB storage, Cosmos DB 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB, AKS cluster management free (node charges apply), API Management 1M calls/mo (Consumption), AI Search 50 MB storage / 10,000 docs / 3 indexes, Static Web Apps 100 GB bandwidth, Logic Apps 4,000 built-in actions, DevOps 5 users, IoT Hub 8,000 msgs/day, plus Azure OpenAI and Microsoft Foundry model access. After the 12 months or credit exhaustion, Microsoft emails you to choose pay-as-you-go or have the subscription disabled; if you convert, free monthly amounts continue.
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time university students only; sign up with a school (.edu) email; one account per person; NO credit card required (explicitly stated on the page). No minimum age stated on the page, but the Azure-for-Students credit offer in the GitHub pack is 18+ (younger students get limited access). No country list published on the page (US-locale content).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students, click 'Start free' (go.microsoft.com/fwlink link id 2062405). 2) Sign up with your school email; verification is via the school email — no card. 3) The $100 credit and free monthly allowances are granted immediately. 4) After account creation, download free dev tools from the Azure Education Hub. Renew yearly while enrolled.
  - *Expiry:* Credit usable within 12 months of account creation; free monthly allowances: 12 months for the 'popular services' set, ongoing (no limit) for the 65+ always-free set. Subscription renewable annually while still a full-time student. No sunset date published.
  - *Gotchas:* The $100 credit is a cap, not cash; once exhausted you must pay unless you stay within the free-allowance limits. Free-tier caps (e.g., VMs 750 hrs, not 730) are hard limits — overage is billed. Data egress, storage over 32 GB on DBs, and premium-tier usage are not covered. You must convert to pay-as-you-go to keep running after credit expiry. The AI free allowances are rebranded under Microsoft Foundry ('Foundry Tools'/'Control Plane') in 2025-2026 — same services, new names. One account per person; no stacking with the Azure Free account's $200.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Azure Free Account (standard tier, non-student) — $200 credit / 30 days + 12-month free services** (high) — Verified today on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free: $200 credit usable on Azure services within 30 days (one per new customer; unused credit stays valid for the full 30 days), free monthly amounts on 20+ popular services for 12 months, and 65+ always-free services ongoing (same lists as the student page: 750 hrs VMs, 1M Functions requests, Cosmos DB 1000 RU/s + 25 GB, etc.). A phone number plus credit/debit card (non-prepaid; credit cards only in HK and Brazil) is required at signup, with a temporary ~$1 authorization that is reversed.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only; one free account with the $200 credit per person. Credit card required (unlike the student track). No funding or company requirements — open to individuals.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free, 'Get started'. 2) Sign in with a Microsoft or GitHub account. 3) Provide phone number + credit/debit card for identity verification. 4) $200 credit activates immediately; 12-month free service amounts apply to new-customer services.
  - *Expiry:* Credit: 30 days from account creation. 20+ free monthly amounts: 12 months (new customers). 65+ always-free services: ongoing. After expiry, Microsoft emails you; if you don't convert to pay-as-you-go, the account and services are disabled.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required here (not for students). After 30 days the $200 lapses; only the free monthly allowances survive. The $25/75-day extension that older guides mention is no longer advertised on the current page. Spending protection applies only while on the free account; convert to PAYG and all usage is billed.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free
- **Microsoft for Startups — single-path program, $200 immediate credits, up to $150,000 via milestones (current 2026 structure)** (high) — Verified today on learn.microsoft.com (page updated 2026-08-05; June 2, 2026 Microsoft blog 'a clearer path from AI development to enterprise growth'): startups apply directly at startups.microsoft.com and immediately get up to $200 in Startup credits (sign up for Azure + verify identity with a personal LinkedIn account; business accounts rejected at this step). Credits grow via automatic milestones: $5,000 after business verification (registered legal entity); $25,000 after 5+ active Azure workloads sustained ~60 days (one-time 2-year credit-use extension granted at this tier); $50,000 at 7+ workloads; $150,000 at 10+ workloads AND ~$3,000/month sustained usage. You can skip tiers; a 10-character investor referral code (from a Microsoft for Startups Investor Network VC/accelerator/partner university) unlocks higher tiers faster, a dedicated Microsoft point of contact, and extra credits. Includes Azure AI/Foundry model access, Marketplace/co-sell benefits, expert guidance.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage B2B software startups: pre-seed through Series B (no Series C funding or later), privately held for-profit, building a software product you own, HQ in a country where Azure is available; NOT more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits; no educational institutions/government/consultancies; no crypto mining. Apply with a personal consumer email (school/work emails rejected). Decisions typically within 3 business days.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to https://startups.microsoft.com, click 'Get started now'. 2) Sign in with a personal Microsoft account (create if needed). 3) Choose path: enter your 10-character investor referral code, or 'Continue without a code'. 4) Verify with LinkedIn to 'Unlock $200 in Azure credits', then complete the 3-step Azure account setup (profile, billing address, payment info). 5) Credits appear in the MFS portal / Azure portal (joins on or after May 29, 2026 activate through the Azure portal; earlier joins used the MFS portal).
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable over the program period; at the $25K milestone you receive a one-time 2-year extension on using credits. Higher tiers require sustained ~60-day workload usage (each workload must stay above $1 spend for all 60 days; the clock starts once the required number of workloads each have >=$1 spend). No fixed sunset date published.
  - *Gotchas:* A credit cap, not cash: money is only 'free' while inside the ceiling, and exceeding it moves you to pay-as-you-go. Payment info/card is required at activation (won't be charged unless you exceed credits and stay on PAYG). Milestone credits are conditional on real sustained usage, not activation — many startups stop at $5K. School/edu emails are explicitly rejected for signup (contrast with the student program). The $150K figures on the still-live Founders Hub signup page and the $10K/$350K caps reflect different (legacy vs current) rules — the Learn program docs are authoritative. Pegasus (invitation-only) program continues separately.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/getting-started-mfs
- **Azure for Startups self-service credits — $1,000 (90 days) + up to $4,000 after business verification** (medium) — Listed on the current Azure for Students page (fetched today) as a separate sign-up tier: $1,000 instant Azure credit valid 90 days, plus up to $4,000 more that remain available for 180 days after business verification. This is the self-serve track of the July 2025 MFS restructure (replacing the old $150K/$10K Founders tiers for un-backed startups), which Microsoft further streamlined in 2026 into the milestone model ($200 start -> $5K verified -> $25K/$50K/$150K milestones).
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startups without investor backing, new to Azure; credit card required for identity verification (only charged if you move to pay-as-you-go beyond credits). Business verification = registered legal entity. Same MFS constraints apply (for-profit, software product, HQ in an Azure-available country, funding/credit caps per current program docs: no Series C+, <=$350K lifetime free Azure credits).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for the 'Azure for Startups' tier from azure.microsoft.com (offered alongside the students and free accounts), or apply at startups.microsoft.com and complete business verification to unlock the additional $4,000. Identity-verification card required.
  - *Expiry:* Base $1,000 credit: 90 days from signup. Additional $4,000: available for 180 days after business verification. Existing credits from before the July 2025 program change remained valid until their original expiry.
  - *Gotchas:* No dedicated support on the self-serve track (self-service only) — the dedicated-Microsoft-POC/support path requires an Investor Network referral. Card required (unlike the student tier). The $4,000 is gated on actually completing business verification within the window. Note the 2026 milestone docs show some tiers now framed as $200/$5,000 gates, so the $1K/$4K structure coexists with the milestone model — check which offer is presented at signup.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Azure Dev Tools for Teaching / Visual Studio Academic Program — free education licenses (Visual Studio, SQL Server, Windows Server, Visio, Office, GitHub benefits)** (medium) — Verified today via the Azure for Students page, which states that after creating the student account you 'download free dev tools from the Azure Education Hub' through the Azure Dev Tools for Teaching program. The page's DFTT bundle lists: Visual Studio (Community, for Mac, plus Agents/Remote Tools), Azure DevOps Server, R Client/Server, SQL Server Developer/Mobile Report Publisher/Standard, System Center, Visio Professional, Windows Server, Skype for Business Server. Schools (accredited institutions) get the teaching licenses; students get the student-side dev licenses for non-commercial, educational use while enrolled.
  - *Eligibility:* Accredited educational institutions (faculty) and enrolled students; claimed through school-issued email / institutional account for the teaching side; the student side rides on the verified Azure for Students account. Non-commercial educational use only.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Verify with your school email on the Azure for Students page (no card). 2) After account creation, open the Azure Education Hub linked there and download the dev-tool licenses. 3) Institutions obtain teaching kits via the Microsoft Academic Program (historically partner.microsoft.com/academic-program; that page returned 403 to today's fetch).
  - *Expiry:* Licenses last while you remain an enrolled student / faculty member; renewed yearly with the student subscription. No fixed sunset.
  - *Gotchas:* Dev-tools licenses are non-commercial — shipping a paid product with them breaches terms. The exact SKU list on the partner.microsoft.com DFTT page couldn't be fetched (403), so treat the granular list as 'per the students page snapshot today'. Office/M365 is generally delivered through the school's campus license (M365 Education) rather than a personal free download.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **GitHub Student Developer Pack (Microsoft-subsidized offers inside the pack: $100 Azure credit, GitHub Pro, Copilot, Codespaces)** (high) — Verified today on github.com/education: verified students (13+, enrolled in a degree/diploma-granting program, school email or dated proof of enrollment) get free GitHub Pro (unlimited private repos, 3,000 Actions minutes/mo, 180 Codespaces hours/mo, 2 GB Packages storage), free GitHub Copilot for Students (300 premium requests/mo class allowance per current plan), plus 80+ partner offers. Microsoft's own offers inside the pack: $100 Azure credit (18+; separate limited access for 13-17), and a 'Microsoft Azure (for ages 13-17)' offer; partner offers include JetBrains, Heroku $13/mo x 24 months, MongoDB $50, domains. Student validation lasts 2 years, then revalidation required. Offers can't be reset by re-verifying; partner terms can limit age/country.
  - *Eligibility:* Enrolled students 13+ with school-issued email or enrollment proof; separate validation from the .edu-only Azure for Students program (documents accepted: ID, transcript, class schedule, verification letter). No credit card for the GitHub side; some partner offers require a card on their own site.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to github.com/education/students (or education.github.com/pack). 2) Start an application under Education benefits settings in your GitHub profile. 3) Submit proof of enrollment; approval lands in the GitHub Education portal. 4) Claim Microsoft offers (Azure $100 credit 18+, etc.) from the pack page.
  - *Expiry:* Valid while student status holds (revalidated every 2 years; the pack is typically held until end of 4th year of study). Individual partner offers have their own durations (e.g., Heroku 24 months, GitKraken 6 months + 80% off).
  - *Gotchas:* Offers are partner-controlled and change or drop out (see changelog: DigitalOcean's $200 credit sunset July 31, 2026; Bump.sh offer ended Dec 9, 2025; 1Password expanded to 2 years in Oct 2026). The $100 Azure credit is the SAME offer as Azure for Students (claim one, not both — one per person). Copilot student entitlements 'are evolving in 2026' per the official discussion; check the live allowance on activation. This is a GitHub program — included here because the Microsoft offers (Azure credit, and DFTT-tied tooling) flow through it.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/education/students
- **2025–26 change:** SUNSET/CHANGES verified as of 2026-10-22: (1) The classic student software bundle storefront bundles.microsoft.com no longer resolves at all (DNS NXDOMAIN), and microsoft.com/en-us/education/bundles, /education/university, and store.office.com/en-us/bundles all 404 — the 'free 1-year bundle worth $199 (Office/Visio/Project/MATLAB)' and '$649 1-year bundle at a $99 student price (Windows 11 + Office + Visual Studio + Team)' cannot be confirmed on any live official page today; the current student hub (microsoft.com/en-us/education/students) no longer lists them and the bundle value proposition was absorbed into the 50%-off M365 Personal + 2026 college-PC promo (M365 Premium) + Azure Dev Tools for Teaching (which still carries free Visio Professional/Project Professional while enrolled). Treat the $199/$649 bundles as sunfetched/consolidated in 2025–2026; confidence medium (no live page to cite, negative evidence from dead domains). (2) The 2026 'Ultimate College Bundle' (M365 Premium + Game Pass + controller with a Windows PC) officially ended: validity 4/15/2026–8/31/2026, redemption through 9/30/2026 — page now says 'This promotion has ended' (a new back-to-school variant is likely to recur in 2027). (3) Azure for Startups has a new shape: the advertised tiers are $1,000 instant / up to $5,000 with 90-day and 180-day credit clocks (vs. the legacy Founders Program's application-based multi-month grants), and the startup entry point is now the Azure Portal startup home view; the marketing site still cites 'up to $150,000 in credits' as the ceiling. (4) The free 12-month M365 Personal-for-US-college-students offer (announced 2025-09-04, initial signup window through 10/31/2025, expanded to more markets in Oct 2025) — its 2026 availability could not be confirmed from the live page (JS-rendered/timeout); the standing, confirmed offer is the 50% student discount. (5) GitHub pack partner lineup churned: ~83 partner entries now; Gitpod, Replit, DigitalOcean, Codeship no longer appear; Copilot Student (free) and GitHub Pro (3,000 Actions minutes/mo, 180 Codespaces hrs/mo) are the core standing benefits. (6) store.office.com is 404 (Office bundles store discontinued); M365 deals now live under microsoft.com/microsoft-365/college-student-pricing.
- **Critic correction** (Microsoft for Startups): Outdated. The 2025 investor-referral model was relaunched in June 2026. Startups now apply directly and get up to $200 in Startup credits immediately. Credits can grow to $150,000 through verified milestones: $5,000 after business verification, then $25,000 (5+ Azure workloads over about 60 days), $50,000 (7+ workloads), and $150,000 (10+ workloads plus about $3,000/month usage). No referral is needed for that path; investor-network startups get extra benefits.

### Microsoft Azure

- **Azure Free Account — $200 credit + 12 months of free services** (high) — $200 Azure credit usable within the first 30 days (forfeited if unused; not banked/transferable). Plus 12 months of free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services and 65+ 'always-free' services, including some AI/Foundry tools: Azure Speech in Foundry Tools (0.5M neural chars/mo, always), Content Safety in Foundry Control Plane (5,000 text records or images/mo, always), Document Intelligence in Foundry Tools (500 pages S0, 12 mo), Azure Vision (5,000 transactions S1/S2/S3, 12 mo), AI Search, Azure SQL DB (100,000 vCore-sec serverless/mo + 32GB), 750 h/mo of B1s/B2pts v2/B2ats v2 VMs. No free LLM/OpenAI token tier. After 30 days or credit exhaustion you must move to pay-as-you-go to keep the 12-month free amounts; overage beyond free monthly amounts then bills P2P.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only (never had an Azure account / 12-mo free services). Individual or org. 12-month free services NOT available to customers signing up directly for P2P in China and India. Country/region is locked at sign-up and cannot be changed later.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to azure.microsoft.com/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account. 2) Choose 'Azure free account'. 3) Complete the 3-step account setup (profile, billing address, payment card). 4) A spending limit is on by default; you immediately get $200/30 days + 12-mo free amounts. 5) To keep free services after 30 days, remove the spending limit (convert to P2P) — only usage above the free amounts is then charged.
  - *Expiry:* $200 credit expires at the end of day 30. The 12-month free-service amounts end at 12 months (the 65+ 'always-free' services continue). Inactive accounts (>90 days) may have compute resources removed with 30 days' notice.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required at signup (temporary $1 authorization; non-prepaid card). It is a trial-then-pay offer, not a perpetual free tier. Services not on the free list (notably Azure OpenAI/LLM tokens, and egress) bill immediately. Unused $200 is lost. Free quantities don't roll over month to month.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit / 12 months, no credit card** (high) — $100 Azure credit valid for 12 months (usable on most Azure products, incl. Azure OpenAI after you apply for access), plus 25+ free services, free monthly amounts of 20+ popular + 65+ always-free services, and free software downloads (Visual Studio Community/Enterprise, SQL Server, Windows Server, R, Visio, Windows 10/11 VMs without needing a Windows 11 Enterprise license). Renewable every year while still a student — each successful renewal grants a fresh $100 and a new 12-month term. A no-credit-card 'Azure for Students Starter' variant also exists.
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time students age 18+ at an accredited 2- or 4-year degree-granting institution. Must verify academic status via school/institution email (.edu or org domain) or via GitHub Student Developer Pack. Not available for MOOCs or for-profit professional training. One subscription per eligible customer, non-transferable, can't be combined with other offers.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to azure.microsoft.com/free/students. 2) Sign up with your school email (or GitHub Student Pack) to verify student status — no credit card. 3) Receive the $100 credit + 25+ services + software downloads. 4) To use Azure OpenAI/LLM, separately apply for access in the AI Foundry portal. 5) Renew annually by re-verifying status (reminder email ~30 days before the anniversary).
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit valid 12 months; renewable yearly while eligible (a new $100 + new 12-month term per renewal). If you exhaust the credit before 12 months the subscription disables until you upgrade to P2P. Deployed resources are decommissioned at term end; you have a 90-day window to upgrade to P2P to keep them.
  - *Gotchas:* No credit card required, but continuing after the credit means pay-as-you-go. There is no dedicated free LLM tier — OpenAI/LLM usage is application-based and consumes your $100 credit. Cannot be combined with other offers. Inactivity >90 days may deprovision compute. Remaining credit is tracked in the Azure Sponsorships portal.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Azure for Startups (self-serve, no investor code) — $1,000 instantly, up to $5,000 total** (high) — For new-Azure-customer startups without an investor referral: $1,000 in Azure credits instantly, no application; plus up to $4,000 more after business verification = up to $5,000 total. Initial $1,000 credits valid 90 days; post-verification credits valid 180 days. Access to the full Azure catalog up to credit limits. This self-serve track can later grow into the Microsoft for Startups / Founders Hub program (up to $150,000) as you demonstrate adoption.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers / early-stage B2B startups; privately held, for-profit; HQ in a country where Azure is available; not an educational institution, government, or consultancy; no cryptocurrency mining; must use a personal consumer Microsoft account (work/school email rejected). Credit card required for identity verification and for any usage beyond the credits.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at startups.microsoft.com ('Get started now') or the Azure for Startups signup on azure.microsoft.com/free/students footer. 2) Choose 'Continue without a code' / 'Build your startup', verify (e.g., via LinkedIn), then complete the 3-step Azure account setup (profile, address, payment card) → $1,000/90 days active. 3) Complete business verification to unlock up to +$4,000 (180 days). 4) If you later obtain an investor referral code, apply for the investor track.
  - *Expiry:* Self-serve: $1,000 = 90 days; +$4,000 = 180 days (post-verification). Credits auto-convert to pay-as-you-go when used or expired. (When on the Founders-Hub track: activate credits within 90 days of accepting the program; once activated, valid up to 2 years, no extensions.)
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required (identity verification + non-covered services + P2P transition). Overage beyond credits bills P2P. Country/region locked at signup. Framing discrepancy: the MfS 'application' page shows a '$200' no-code starter and '$1,000' for the code path, while the FAQ/self-serve offer states '$1,000 (90d) + up to $4,000 (180d)'; treat $1,000 as the current no-investor baseline. Credits are tied to the specific MSA/subscription and cannot be transferred.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/mfs-faqs
- **Microsoft for Startups / Founders Hub (investor-referred) — start ~$100,000, expand to up to $150,000** (high) — Startups with a 10-character referral code from a Microsoft Investor Network investor/accelerator/VC start with ~$100,000 in Azure credits and can unlock up to $150,000 total over time based on business verification, service adoption, and sustained Azure usage. The investor offer also bundles third-party benefits: GitHub Enterprise, LinkedIn Premium Business, Microsoft 365 Business Premium, Visual Studio Enterprise, Dynamics 365, Power Platform, plus Azure Standard Support and a dedicated MfS point of contact. OpenAI/Foundry (LLM) usage is covered by the Azure credits. Program is open-ended on time; you reapply/requalify as you grow.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-seed through Series B (NOT Series C or later). Privately held, for-profit B2B software/AI startup; working toward/at MVP. Must NOT have received more than $350,000 in lifetime free Azure credits. HQ in a country where Azure is available. Not an educational institution, government, consultancy, or agency; no crypto mining. Must use a personal consumer email (work/school/company-domain email rejected). Referral code comes from an Investor Network member (list not public).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Get a 10-character investor referral code from your investor/accelerator/VC (Microsoft doesn't publish the list). 2) At startups.microsoft.com, 'Get started now', sign in with a personal Microsoft account, enter the code, verify contact email + phone (SMS). 3) Submit business info (registered name matching legal docs, industry, website, address). 4) 3-step Azure account setup with a payment card → $1,000 starter active while reviewed. 5) Review ~3 business days; on approval, credits expand up to $150,000. 6) Activate credits within 90 days of accepting the program agreement.
  - *Expiry:* Activate within 90 days of accepting the program agreement or the offer expires (reapply). Once activated, Azure credits valid up to 2 years; extensions are not available. Auto-convert to P2P when credits run out.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card required. Benefits (GitHub, M365, VS Enterprise, etc.) must be redeemed on the SAME personal Microsoft account and NOT on an existing enterprise account — 'Already redeemed' errors are common. Credits can't be transferred across accounts. Declined applicants may reapply after 14 days. The $350,000 lifetime free-Azure-credit cap and the Series-C ceiling are hard disqualifiers. Country/region locked at signup.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview
- **Azure OpenAI / Microsoft Foundry (Azure AI Foundry) — no free LLM tier; paid + application-based** (high) — There is NO free monthly tier or free API-call allowance for OpenAI/LLM models on Azure (now branded 'Azure AI Foundry' / 'Microsoft Foundry', with sub-brands Foundry Tools, Foundry Control Plane, Foundry IQ). Pricing is pay-as-you-go per input/output token, or provisioned throughput units (PTUs) with monthly/annual reservations; the Batch API returns completions within 24h at ~50% discount. The only 'free' way to get LLM tokens is to burn the credits from the other offers above ($200 free account / $100 student / $1,000-$5,000 startup). Other Foundry tools DO have free allowances (Speech 0.5M chars/mo always-free; Content Safety 5,000/mo; Document Intelligence 500 pages/12mo; Vision 5,000/12mo).
  - *Eligibility:* Requires an Azure account plus a payment method; model deployments are application-based (apply for access, specify use case/responsible-AI mitigations; historically gated by demand). Available only in selected regions per model.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Hold one of the credit offers above (or P2P). 2) In the Azure portal, create an Azure AI Foundry resource. 3) Request access to a model (e.g., GPT) by submitting a use case. 4) Deploy the model; token usage draws down your free credits first, then bills P2P.
  - *Gotchas:* Not a genuine free LLM tier — 'free' only via borrowed credits; once credits exhaust it is pay-as-you-go. Per-model/per-region deployment quotas and limits apply; high-demand models may be gated. Data egress and extra meters add cost. Rebrand means 'Azure OpenAI' docs/prices now live under 'Microsoft Foundry'.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/cognitive-services/openai-service/
- **Student developer tools — Visual Studio (Community/Enterprise), GitHub Student Pack, Azure Dev/Test credits** (medium) — Azure for Students includes free software downloads (Visual Studio Community/Enterprise, SQL Server Developer/Standard, Windows Server, R, Visio Professional, Windows 10/11 VMs). Eligible students get Visual Studio Enterprise with ~$150/month of Azure Dev/Test credits (renewable; no payment method needed — usage auto-stops when the monthly credit is consumed); Visual Studio Professional gives ~$50/month. The GitHub Student Developer Pack (free, .edu) bundles GitHub Copilot, VS Code tools, Databricks and more. 'Dev Tools for Teaching' extends software access to faculty/students of enrolled institutions.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified students (.edu or GitHub Student Pack). Visual Studio Enterprise for students is available through eligible programs (e.g., Student Ambassadors); the Dev/Test credits are strictly for development/test (non-production). GitHub Student Pack requires a .edu email. Dev Tools for Teaching is institution-licensed.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Verify student status (.edu / GitHub Student Pack) via Azure for Students. 2) Download Visual Studio / dev tools from your Visual Studio subscription benefits portal. 3) Claim the GitHub Student Developer Pack at github.com/student (free, .edu). 4) If on an eligible VS plan, activate the individual Azure Dev/Test credit subscription (no payment method; usage stops at the monthly cap).
  - *Gotchas:* Dev/Test credits are void if used for production. Microsoft no longer sells new Visual Studio Enterprise/Professional ANNUAL cloud subscriptions (existing customers can renew; new users go to visualstudio.microsoft.com/vs/pricing). GitHub Pack items carry their own terms/caps (e.g., Copilot free tier for students). Monthly Dev/Test credit does not roll over.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/subscriptions/vs-azure-eligibility
- **Azure for Students (higher-ed): $100 Azure credit + free services, no credit card** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students and the official offer page (ms-azr-0170p). Students get $100 in Azure credits to use within 12 months plus: free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (new Azure customers only), free monthly amounts of 65+ always-free services, access to the full Azure service catalog up to free amounts + the $100 credit, and access to Azure OpenAI and Microsoft Foundry with 1,700+ foundation models (OpenAI o1/GPT-4 series, Hugging Face, Meta, Cohere, Phi SLMs). Includes free 'Software Download Benefits' via the Azure Education Hub: Visual Studio (Community/Code/for Mac, Agents for VS), SQL Server Developer & Standard, Windows Server, Visio Professional, R Client/Server, Hyper-V Remote Tools, System Center, Azure DevOps Server, SQL Server Mobile Report Publisher. Renew annually while a student: each renewal gives a fresh $100 + another 12-month term (confirmed by MS staff in a March 2026 Q&A and the official FAQ; reminder emails ~30 days before expiry; no hard cap on renewals).
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time students at an accredited degree-granting 2- or 4-year university; affirm age 18+; verify academic status via your organization's (school) email address — Shibboleth where supported, or GitHub Student Developer Pack verification code as fallback; NOT available for MOOCs or for-profit professional training; one subscription per person; 'limited supply of activations, while supplies last'; not available in sovereign-cloud (US Gov, China, Germany) regions; offer available where Azure is commercially available.
  - *How to claim:* Go to azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students → sign up with a Microsoft or GitHub account → verify student status with school email (or GitHub code at signup.azure.com/studentverification?offerType=1) → accept offer terms; no credit card at sign-up. Track remaining credit in the Azure Sponsorships portal. After 12 months, re-sign up for the offer to renew; to renew you re-verify status.
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit and 12-month free services window run from sign-up; renew annually while eligible. If credit is exhausted early the subscription is DISABLED (you can upgrade to Pay-As-You-Go via support; deployed resources are then decommissioned at the end of the 12-month term — 90 days to convert). Invoicing statements may appear monthly but are covered by the credit.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits cannot be used on Azure Marketplace offers and don't carry over between months or subscriptions; unused credit is forfeited at 12 months; if you previously held any Azure free trial/student offer on the same Microsoft identity you cannot sign up again (you'll hit a 'no access' wall and must convert that old sub to PAYG); the offer can be terminated at Microsoft's discretion; the 20+ free-for-12-months services apply only to NEW Azure customers; anything beyond free amounts deducts from the $100; the 'Visual Studio Pro for 12 months' item in many older guides is NOT in this offer anymore — Pro only comes from the separate GitHub Student Developer Pack; keep at least one resource/portal activity so the sub doesn't lapse as inactive (MS community advises 90-day activity, not official).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students(fetched2026-10-22;offerterms:https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0170p;FAQ:https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/faq)
- **Azure for Students Starter (K-12 path): free core-service tiers, no credit card, $0 spend limit** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 (official offer page ms-azr-0144p + Learn doc 'About the Azure for Students Starter program'). A second, lighter Azure student path — the same free/students page says 'Try Azure for Students or Azure for Students Starter — no credit card required.' Gives a verified K-12 (secondary/high-school) student free-tier access with a $0 spending limit to: Azure App Service, Azure Functions, Azure Notification Hubs, Azure Database for MySQL (in-app), Application Insights (Azure Monitor), and Azure DevOps. No commitment or time limit stated on the offer page; one subscription per eligible customer; not transferable; can't be combined with other offers; not for MOOCs/for-profit training; upgrade to Pay-As-You-Go at any time for the full catalog. (A Russian-localized Learn copy says the sub lasts a year and must be renewed — the en-US offer page governs: no time limit.)
  - *Eligibility:* Verified K-12 students; affirm age 13+ if residing in the US, 16+ if residing elsewhere; verify academic status through your organization's email (or Shibboleth where supported); no credit card.
  - *How to claim:* Enroll at the Azure for Students Starter offer page (azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0144p, linked from the 'Choose the right Azure account' section of azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students) → verify with school email/Shibboleth → account starts with a $0 spend limit; upgrade in the Azure portal to PAYG when you need more.
  - *Expiry:* Offer page: no commitment or time limit. Upgrade path: Pay-As-You-Go anytime.
  - *Gotchas:* $0 spending limit means you can ONLY use the free tiers of the listed services — everything else is blocked, not billed; limited to six service families (no VMs, no OpenAI models, no credits); one per customer; prior Azure trial on the same identity blocks signup.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0144p(plushttps://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/azure-dev-tools-teaching/azure-students-starter-program)
- **Microsoft for Startups, self-service track: $1,000 Azure credits instantly (90 days), up to $5,000 (180 days) after business verification** (high) — Materially changed in 2025-2026. As of 2026-10-22, the 'Azure for Startups' card on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students (fetched today) states: $1,000 in Azure credits instantly, no application required; unlock up to $5,000 in total credits with business verification; credits valid 90 days (initial) and 180 days (post-verification); full Azure catalog up to credit limits; 'spending protection — credit card required only for identity verification and services beyond credit'; no upfront commitment, cancel anytime, moves to pay-as-you-go after credits expire. This replaced the old Founders Hub on July 1, 2025. As of June 2026 (official MS blog + Learn overview, updated 2026-08-05): the program is now single-path — apply at startups.microsoft.com, start building immediately with Startup credits while eligibility is verified, and 'unlock up to $150K in Startup credits over time' based on verified progress, service adoption, and sustained Azure usage; applications typically reviewed in 3 business days; LLM/AI access is via Microsoft Foundry (GPT-4-class models, 1,700+ foundation models).
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only; privately held, for-profit company developing a software-based product it owns; headquartered in a country where Azure is available; NOT an educational institution/government/consultancy/agency; no cryptocurrency mining; NOT raised Series C funding or later; NOT previously received more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits (updated cap; the legacy Founders Hub signup page still shows a $10,000 cap — the current Learn doc governs). A credit card is required (unlike the student offers) — only for identity verification and for any services/usage beyond the credits.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at startups.microsoft.com ('Get started') — the $1,000 is granted instantly with no application/verification wait; complete the business-verification checklist to unlock the remaining $4,000 (credits then valid 180 days); enter an Investor Network referral code during application if you have one. Manage/activate credits and benefits in the Azure Portal. After credits expire you move to pay-as-you-go; cancel anytime.
  - *Expiry:* $1,000 valid 90 days from sign-up; $5,000 tier valid 180 days from verification. No renewals; expiration converts the sub to PAYG. June 2026 change: further credits up to $150K are unlocked incrementally 'over time' based on service adoption/sustained usage (not a fixed grant).
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required here (identity verification) — unlike the student offers; no dedicated technical support on the self-service track (that's the Investor-Network benefit); the 90/180-day windows are short — if you can't burn $5k in 6 months the account flips to PAYG; Microsoft can change/terminate the offer (as it did on 2025-07-01 with little notice); legacy Founders-Hub 'levels' (Expert/Champion Networks) no longer exist.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students(fetched2026-10-22);programoverview:https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview;changeannouncement:https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/blog/build-fast-scale-smart-and-sell-more-microsoft-for-startups-evolves-to-better-serve-the-startup-ecosystem/
- **Microsoft for Startups, Investor Network track: referral-gated, $100,000+ Azure credits + dedicated Microsoft point of contact** (medium) — Per the July 1, 2025 restructuring (The Register/TechRadar coverage + official blog) and the June 2, 2026 official blog: startups referred by a Microsoft for Startups Investor Network partner (a select group of VCs, accelerators, and universities) enter a 'higher touch' track that 'starts with $100,000 in Azure credits' (press figures; the official Learn overview describes it less precisely as 'higher-value benefits earlier', 'additional Startup credits, expanded support, and third-party benefits'). Includes dedicated Microsoft POC, deeper Microsoft Marketplace and co-sell support, and invitation-only Pegasus program (dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect) which Microsoft explicitly kept unchanged. The old Founders Hub tiers up to $150K and the Expert/Champion Networks were retired; existing activated legacy credits stayed valid until their expiry, then auto-converted to PAYG.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup must be tied to an Investor Network-affiliated VC/accelerator/university that issues you a referral code, entered during the startups.microsoft.com application; same company-level eligibility as the self-service track (private, for-profit, software product, no Series C+, HQ in an Azure region).
  - *How to claim:* Get the referral code from your investor/accelerator partner → apply at startups.microsoft.com and enter the code → you are routed to the enhanced track instead of the $1,000/$5,000 self-serve ramp.
  - *Expiry:* Credit validity follows program terms (legacy $100K-tier grants were ~180-day windows per level; new grants track the 'credits over time' model). Pegasus is invitation-only and ongoing.
  - *Gotchas:* The exact $100,000 figure is from press, not the current official Learn page (which I fetched today) — treat as 'about $100K+'; no funding-amount cap like AWS Activate, but the Series C+ and $350K lifetime-free-credit caps disqualify most well-funded companies; if your legacy Founders Hub credits expired without investor affiliation, your sub already converted to PAYG.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/blog/build-fast-scale-smart-and-sell-more-microsoft-for-startups-evolves-to-better-serve-the-startup-ecosystem/;https://www.theregister.com/off-prem/2025/07/01/microsoft-changes-conditions-for-azure-startup-credits/101398;https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview
- **Azure Free account (also open to students without .edu verification): $200 credit for 30 days + 12-month and always-free services** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/. For any new Azure customer (incl. students who don't have a qualifying school email): $200 credit usable within 30 days; free amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (750 h/mo B1s + B2pts v2 Arm + B2ats v2 AMD VMs; Azure SQL serverless 100,000 vCore-sec/mo + 32 GB; PostgreSQL & MySQL Flexible 750 h/mo B1MS + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup; Cosmos DB 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB; Container Registry Standard 100 GB; SQL Managed Instance 720 vCore-h + 64 GB data + 64 GB backup; Vision 5,000 txns/tier) plus 65+ always-free services (DevOps 5 users, Functions 1M requests, App Service 10 apps/1 GB/1 h-day, Static Web Apps 100 GB bandwidth, Cosmos/DocumentDB 32 GB, IoT Hub 8,000 msg/day, Logic Apps 4,000 actions, Notification Hubs 1M, AI Search 50 MB/10k docs/3 indexes, Document Intelligence 500 pages S0, Translator 2M chars, Content Safety 5,000 records, Speech 0.5M neural chars/mo, Container Apps 180k vCPU-s/360k GiB-s/2M reqs, API Mgmt 1M calls/mo, Azure Maps 1,000–5,000 txns, AKS management free). LLM/AI: Azure OpenAI and Microsoft Foundry models accessible with the $200 (or student $100); the OpenAI 'free trial' token allowances (historically ~1,000 gpt-4o-mini tokens/day, 40k/mo, ~3 months; 50/day gpt-4o, o1, gpt-4; 500/day gpt-3.5-turbo) could not be re-verified today because the pricing tables render via JS and returned placeholders — treat exact token figures as unconfirmed. One free-credit account per person; not available in US Gov/China/Germany sovereign clouds.
  - *Eligibility:* Any NEW Azure customer (students, startups, individuals). Requires a phone number + a credit/debit card (non-prepaid) — card used for identity verification with a small reversed authorization hold; in Hong Kong and Brazil only credit cards are accepted; billing country must be in the supported list (~140 countries/regions). A startup on this path uses the $200 trial PLUS the $1,000→$5,000 Startup credits on a separate offer.
  - *How to claim:* azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/ → 'Try Azure for free' → sign in with Microsoft or GitHub account → enter phone + card → $200 credit and free tiers activate immediately. After 30 days, if you don't convert to PAYG, the account is disabled (unused credit is kept only if you convert early).
  - *Expiry:* $200 credit: 30 days. 12-month free amounts: 12 months from sign-up, then billed at PAYG. Always-free services: ongoing, no expiry. No renewals.
  - *Gotchas:* This is the one Azure offer that REQUIRES a credit card — students without .edu can't use it card-free; data egress (internet outbound), inbound-to-public-IP traffic, and any meter beyond the listed free amounts are charged against the $200 and then PAYG; '12-month free' services that are 'always free' are different products — check the Free/Are-You-Aware tabs on each pricing page; sovereign-cloud regions excluded; one per person.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/(fetched2026-10-22)
- **LLM & AI free tiers inside Azure (Microsoft Foundry / Azure AI services) — what students and everyone get at no cost** (medium) — From the official student/free pages fetched 2026-10-22: always-free (all customers, no CC): Azure Speech in Foundry Tools 0.5M neural characters/month; Azure AI Search 50 MB storage / 10,000 docs / 3 indexes per service; Azure Document Intelligence 500 pages S0 tier; Translator 2M characters; Content Safety 5,000 text records or images; Machine Learning 'Free' (no meter); Azure Maps 1,000–5,000 transactions; IoT 8,000 msgs/day. 12-month (new customers): Azure Vision 5,000 transactions each S1/S2/S3. Students additionally get: 'access to industry-leading technology like Azure OpenAI' and 1,700+ foundation models (Microsoft, OpenAI, Hugging Face, Meta, Cohere, incl. Phi small language models) via Microsoft Foundry, billed to their $100 student credit or free quotas; the 'try Azure OpenAI' free-trial token quotas (e.g., gpt-4o-mini 1,000 tokens/day / 40,000/mo for 3 months; 50/day for gpt-4o, o1, gpt-4; 500/day gpt-3.5-turbo) are the long-standing published numbers but I could not re-confirm today (JS-rendered pricing tables returned '$–' placeholders), so treat per-model token quotas as possibly stale. Notably the 2026 pages rebrand Azure AI services as 'Microsoft Foundry'/'Foundry Tools'.
  - *Eligibility:* Always-free tiers: any Azure customer, no credit card. 12-month quotas: new Azure customers. Azure OpenAI models on the student offer: valid Azure for Students subscription (no CC) with access requests/approval per model — access to some frontier models still requires an OpenAI access application stating use case.
  - *How to claim:* Student: sign up for Azure for Students (no CC) → deploy AI resources (Foundry/OpenAI, Speech, AI Search) and stay within free quotas; anything above quotas deducts from the $100. Startup/general: $200 free account or $1,000 Startup credits cover model usage; Foundry Playground offers free trial model calls for experimentation.
  - *Expiry:* Always-free quotas: ongoing monthly allowances. 12-month quotas: end at 12 months. OpenAI free-trial tokens: historically 3 months from account creation (unconfirmed for 2026-10).
  - *Gotchas:* Token-based LLM pricing means the $100/$1,000 credits burn quickly on frontier models (o-series and GPT-6 'Sol/Luna' tiers are the expensive end of the catalog); free-tier F0 quotas per-minute/per-day limits apply; credits can't be used on Marketplace; 'always free' does NOT cover premium tiers of the same service (e.g., Content Safety S1+, Vision S4+); check per-service 'Free' vs 'Are you aware' pricing tabs; egress/data-transfer for media responses is a separate meter.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/studentsandhttps://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/(bothfetched2026-10-22);pricingdetail:https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/azure-openai/
- **Managed databases & data-platform free tiers (usable by students; the 'free tier' front)** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/. Managed-database offers: (always free, all customers) Azure SQL Database serverless 100,000 vCore-seconds/month + 32 GB storage; Azure Cosmos DB dedicated 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB/month free for 12 months then continues as 'Free' tier with 2 free GB storage + 500 RU/s for all customers; DocumentDB dedicated MongoDB cluster 32 GB; Database Migration Service free Standard compute; Azure Data Catalog unlimited users; Data Factory 5 low-frequency activities; Data Explorer free; Query Store free. (12 months, new customers) Azure Database for PostgreSQL Flexible Server 750 h B1MS + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup; Azure Database for MySQL Flexible Server same; SQL Managed Instance 720 vCore-hours (4 or 8 vCore General Purpose) + 64 GB data + 64 GB backup; Azure SQL Database 750 h B5m/baseline + 100 vCore-min burstable. Free backup: 7 days for SQL DB/Managed Instance. These are the main free managed-database options; no CC needed for the student offer that bundles them.
  - *Eligibility:* Always-free database tiers: any Azure customer, no CC. 12-month amounts: new Azure customers only (students get them via the no-CC Azure for Students offer; startups via $1,000→$5,000 Startup credits; everyone else via the $200 CC-required free account).
  - *How to claim:* With a no-CC Azure for Students subscription, deploy PostgreSQL/MySQL Flexible Server B1MS or the SQL serverless database directly from the portal within the free amounts — no card, no charge until you exceed them; students can use up to the $100 credit for overflow.
  - *Expiry:* Always-free: ongoing (Cosmos free tier is a permanent tier, not 12-month). 12-month amounts: end at 12 months then PAYG. Post-12-month Cosmos DB falls to the permanent free 500 RU/s + 2 GB tier, not deletion.
  - *Gotchas:* Free amounts are per-service per-region; backup storage beyond the free 32 GB is billed; egress from VMs/DBs to internet is a separate meter not in the free quota; 'always free' for Cosmos ≠ unlimited — writes beyond 25 GB are blocked or billed; PostgreSQL/MySQL free 750 h is instance-hours (one B1MS instance, 24/7), not multiple; AKS cluster management is free but node VMs and disk are billed; free SQL serverless is capped at 4 vCore serverless pools.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/(fetched2026-10-22)
- **Education licenses: Visual Studio Dev Essentials (student dev subscription; the VS Pro question)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Per vs-azure-eligibility (learn.microsoft.com, fetched 2026-10-22) and university IT pages: Visual Studio subscriptions carry individual Azure Dev/Test credits (Pro $50/mo, Enterprise $150/mo) usable without a payment method; 'Visual Studio Enterprise – Azure Dev Tools for Teaching' no longer exists. Dev Essentials (the successor to the free 'Microsoft Imagine' dev subscription) offers students a Premium tier that bundles up to $200/month Azure credit, Visual Studio Enterprise access, and 10,000 Azure hours of training/tools — students qualify via GitHub/education verification (the exact Premium grant figures come from a university IT mirror, low confidence; official visualstudio.com page returned 404 today). This is how students today get 'Visual Studio Pro/Enterprise-class' IDE licenses for 12 months — NOT via Azure for Students, whose bundled software list is Visual Studio Community/Code (Pro/Enterprise are not in the Azure-for-Students benefit set after the 2025 Dev Tools for Teaching sunset).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified students (or individual developers for Basic tier) — students get the Premium Dev Essentials subscription at no cost via education verification; Basic tier is free to everyone with a Microsoft account.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in at my.visualstudio.com / visualstudio.microsoft.com with a verified student email or GitHub account claiming the student benefit → Dev Essentials Premium activates, including the monthly Azure Dev/Test credit; renew annually while still a student.
  - *Expiry:* Monthly Azure credits renew while the subscription is active (VS subs: renewable, per the eligibility table). GitHub Student Developer Pack route (not Azure, adjacent offer) also grants 12 months of Visual Studio Pro/Enterprise-class licenses plus OpenAI credits — the likely origin of the '12 months of Visual Studio Pro' figure in the known-so-far notes.
  - *Gotchas:* The $200/mo Dev Essentials Azure credit figure is third-party-cited (university IT mirror) and could not be confirmed on the official page today (404) — verify before relying on it; Dev Essentials Basic has no Azure credits; credits stop automatically when the monthly amount is used (no CC on file = auto-stop); 'Pro' IDE via GitHub pack is not an Azure offer and its terms/eligibility are GitHub's.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/subscriptions/vs-azure-eligibility(fetched2026-10-22;officialdev-essentialspagewas404today)
- **Microsoft for Startups — Azure Startup Credits (milestone program, up to $150K)** (high) — Tiered 'Startup credits' on a sponsored Azure subscription: M1 $200 (sign up + verify with a personal LinkedIn account), M2 $5,000 (business verification: registered legal entity), M3 $25,000 (5+ Azure workloads active ~60 days; also grants a one-time 2-year extension to use credits), M4 $50,000 (7+ workloads ~60 days), M5 $150,000 (10+ workloads AND ~$3,000/month Azure usage ~60 days). The 60-day clock starts once each workload has at least $1 of spend; all required workloads must stay above $1/day for the full 60 days. Tiers are NOT sequential — accounts are auto-evaluated and upgraded, so a scaling startup can jump tiers. Credits apply to eligible Azure consumption, select first-party Microsoft Foundry models, and GitHub Copilot/Codespaces/Actions (only if the Azure subscription is connected as the GitHub billing method). When credits are exhausted or expire, the subscription auto-converts to pay-as-you-go.
  - *Eligibility:* B2B software/AI/tech startups, pre-seed through Series B (must NOT have raised Series C or later); privately held, for-profit, not a subsidiary; owns its software product; HQ in a country/region where the program operates (country/region is locked at sign-up and cannot be changed; unsupported country = offer unavailable); not an educational institution, government, consultancy, or agency; not involved in crypto mining; company has NOT received more than $350,000 in lifetime free Azure credits. Sign-in must use a personal consumer Microsoft account — work/school/company-domain emails are rejected.
  - *How to claim:* 1) microsoft.com/en-us/startups → 'Get started now'; 2) sign in with a personal Microsoft account; 3) 'Continue without a code' (or enter a 10-char investor code, see separate finding); 4) verify identity with a personal LinkedIn account; 5) complete 3-step Azure account creation (profile, billing address, payment card); 6) 'Build your startup' dashboard shows $200 unlocked immediately (balance can lag up to 24h); 7) application reviewed in ~3 business days, decision by email; manage credits/portal in Azure Portal and MfS portal.
  - *Expiry:* M3 ($25K) grants a one-time 2-year extension of credit validity; otherwise no fixed validity per tier is published on the pages fetched — when credits are fully used or expired the subscription automatically becomes pay-as-you-go. Set Azure budgets/alerts; 'credits eventually run out' is Microsoft's own framing.
  - *Gotchas:* CREDIT CARD IS REQUIRED (non-optional: card number/expiry/CVV at sign-up; auto-converts to pay-as-you-go when credits run out or expire — no upfront commitment, subscription cancellable). Personal consumer email only. Country/region cannot be changed later. GitHub usage is NOT credit-covered out of the box — you must connect the Azure subscription as a GitHub payment method, and then only metered/eligible GitHub services draw on credits. Third-party (partner/community) Foundry models are separately billed and not covered. Egress and other metered Azure items are still billed through the credits, not free forever.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/getting-started-mfs
- **Microsoft for Startups Investor Network referral code ($1,000 starter credits, up to $200K cap, earlier tier unlocks)** (high) — Startups with a referral from a Microsoft for Startups Investor Network partner (VC/accelerator) enter a 10-character code during application and immediately unlock $1,000 in starter Azure credits instead of $200 while their application is under review. Per the benefits page (updated 2026-06-15), eligible Investor Network-backed startups may unlock up to $200,000 in Startup credits (vs the $150K standard cap) plus expanded support and third-party benefits, and 'may unlock higher-value benefits earlier in their journey'. Microsoft does not publish the list of participating investors.
  - *Eligibility:* Same base program eligibility (pre-seed→Series B B2B software startup etc.), PLUS the referral code from your VC/accelerator. Code is obtained directly from your investor — not issued publicly. Business name/address must match legal documents or business verification fails and acceptance is delayed.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Ask your investor/accelerator for the 10-character code; 2) at microsoft.com/en-us/startups select Get started and enter the code; 3) verify contact email (code expires in 10 min) and phone number; 4) submit business information (registered name, industry, website, legal address); 5) complete Azure account setup — dashboard shows 'We're reviewing your application' with $1,000 in starter credits immediately available; 6) approval (~3 business days) unlocks expanded credits (up to $200K cap) and the full benefits package.
  - *Expiry:* Email verification code expires in 10 minutes. Credit/term rules follow the standard milestone program (2-year extension at the $25K milestone; auto pay-as-you-go on exhaustion).
  - *Gotchas:* Code is not public and investor lists are not shared — ask your accelerator/VC directly. Mismatched legal name/address = failed business verification. The $1,000 is while the application is under review; higher tiers follow the same workload/usage milestones (M3–M5) as the standard track.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/application
- **Microsoft for Startups — free software/tool benefits (M365, GitHub Enterprise, Power BI, Dynamics, LinkedIn, GPU, support)** (high) — Technical benefits table (learn.microsoft.com, updated 2026-08-12): Microsoft 365 Business Premium 50 seats/1 year; GitHub Enterprise 20 seats/1 year; Power BI Pro 10 seats/1 year; Visual Studio 5 seats (until time in the MfS portal ends); Power Apps & Power Automate 10 seats/1 year; Dynamics 365 Customer Service & Sales Enterprise 10 seats/1 year; Dynamics 365 Team Member 25 seats/1 year; LinkedIn Premium Business 1 seat at 75% off for 4 months; 'Startup Cluster' short-term GPU access (ND H100, NDm A100 VMs, subject to availability); higher Azure OpenAI quota limits; ACA serverless GPU quota (2–7 day approval); 24/7 Azure Standard Support (normally ~$1,200/year); dedicated MfS point of contact; go-to-market benefits (Microsoft Marketplace, co-sell).
  - *Eligibility:* Approved Microsoft for Startups members (apply per the program; investor-backed startups get the expanded set). GPU/AI-access items are explicitly 'subject to availability' and gated by support tickets.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in to the MfS portal / Azure Portal after approval; 'find, activate, and manage benefits' per the program docs; GPU VMs: request per Azure quota guidance (or ticket via the MfS portal including the Azure request number, 2–7 days); serverless GPUs: Azure Container Apps → Support ticket; AI limited-access features: formal registration then MfS ticket if not auto-approved.
  - *Expiry:* Software seats: 1 year (Visual Studio: until MfS membership ends). LinkedIn discount: 4 months. GPU access: short-term/availability-based.
  - *Gotchas:* GPU and AI quota benefits are availability-based, not guaranteed. Benefits are 1-year seats and must be re-earned/refreshed with program membership. Credits do not cover non-Azure products, Microsoft Support Plans, or Azure Marketplace purchases.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/benefits/technical-benefits/technical-benefits-overview
- **Azure free account — $200 trial credit + 12-month free services + 65+ always-free services** (high) — Any new customer: $200 credit usable for 30 days; 20+ popular services free for monthly amounts for 12 months (e.g., 750 hrs of B1s/B2s burstable VMs, 750 hrs PostgreSQL/MySQL Flexible Server B1MS + 32GB storage, 720 vCore-hrs SQL Managed Instance, Container Registry 100GB/10 webhooks, plus Azure DevOps 5 users, Functions 1M requests, Cosmos DB 1,000 RU/s + 25GB, App Service up to 10 web/API apps, Static Web Apps 100GB bandwidth + .5GB storage, AKS management free, AI Search 50MB/10,000 docs, etc.); 65+ 'always-free' services (Speech 0.5M neural chars/mo, Bot Service 10K premium msgs, Notification Hubs 1M pushes, API Management 1M calls, etc.) have no time limit. One $200-credit free offer per new customer.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers in one of 140 commercially available countries/regions. Sign-up requires a phone number and a credit or non-prepaid debit card (a ~$1 verification hold may appear and is reversed; credit cards only in Hong Kong and Brazil). Excludes sovereign clouds: US Government, Azure China, Azure Germany. No .edu, no age cap, no startup restriction.
  - *How to claim:* azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free → 'Try Azure for free'; sign up with a Microsoft account (or 'Sign in with GitHub'); if you switch to pay-as-you-go before day 30, remaining credit stays valid for the full 30-day window.
  - *Expiry:* $200 credit: 30 days. Free monthly amounts: 12 months. Always-free tiers: indefinite. Auto-convert/disability to pay-as-you-go after credit expiry.
  - *Gotchas:* CREDIT CARD REQUIRED — the free account is NOT a no-CC offer. After 30 days or credit exhaustion the account is disabled unless converted to pay-as-you-go; the 12-month free service amounts lapse after 12 months (service then bills metered). Data egress and non-free meters are always billed. Trial is 30-day; only the 'always-free' tiers are indefinite.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit + free cloud services, no credit card** (high) — Students get $100 in Azure service credits plus free access to popular cloud services and developer tools (Visual Studio, VS Code, AI/ML/data learning resources), 12-month-style free service access; via the GitHub Student Developer Pack the Azure offer is '25+ cloud services and $100 in Azure credit' for students 18+ (a separate 13–17 track exists). No credit card required and 'no need to enter a credit card' — eligibility is proven with a school email.
  - *Eligibility:* Current, verified students — sign up with your school/academic email; no CC. The GitHub Pack routes 18+ students to the main Azure offer and 13–17 to a separate offer. Faculty offer ($200 in past pages) could not be verified today (education pricing page now 404).
  - *How to claim:* azure.microsoft.com/en-us/education → 'Start free' → verify with school email → $100 credit and free services available; or claim the Azure offer inside the GitHub Student Pack (github.com/settings/education/benefits).
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit: single allotment; free services tied to active student status. No renewal cycle published on the page fetched.
  - *Gotchas:* No CC required (contrast with the $200 free account). Credit is not a subscription; separate from any pay-as-you-go account. Regional availability follows Azure availability.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/education
- **GitHub Student Developer Pack — free GitHub Pro, Copilot, Codespaces + 100+ partner benefits (incl. Microsoft 365/1TB, Azure $100)** (high) — For verified students: free GitHub Pro; unlimited GitHub Copilot completions plus an allowance of GitHub AI Credits with limited chat/agent usage; free Pro-level Codespaces; GitHub Pages (1 site + unlimited project sites); Microsoft Azure $100 credit + 25+ services; Microsoft 365 productivity apps with AI + 1TB OneDrive; JetBrains subscription (renewed annually); MongoDB $50 Atlas credits + free certification; Datadog Pro (10 servers, 2 years free); 1Password free 2 years incl. Developer Tools; Heroku $13/mo for 24 months; Notion Education plan; free domains/SSL from Namecheap/Name.com/.TECH; 6–12 month access to Educative, FrontendMasters, Scrimba, DataCamp, Appwrite; learning bundles organized into 'Experiences' tiers.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified students via the GitHub Education portal (school academic email or institutional proof; 18+ main track, 13–17 separate track). No credit card needed for the pack itself.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in to GitHub → github.com/settings/education → verify student status → claim offers from the benefits page ('All offers' listing, filterable by category).
  - *Expiry:* Per-benefit: 1–24 months (Heroku 24 mo, Datadog/1Password 2 yr, learning platforms 1–12 mo, JetBrains annual). Pack © 2026; 2025–26 changes: new AI-partner additions (Camber, Boot.dev, ToDiagram) and the 'Experiences' bundle layer; several older standalone offers no longer listed.
  - *Gotchas:* Most benefits end when student status ends ('while you are a student'); specific durations vary (1 month–24 months); JetBrains requires annual renewal; Copilot access is 'limited chat and agent usage' under the student allowance, not full Copilot plans.
